then this follows from the following exact sequence

1\to\pi_{0}(T)(F)=\pi_{0}(T)^{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}}}\to\pi_{0}(T)\xrightarrow{t\to\frac{\sigma(t)}{t}}\pi_{0}(T)\to\pi_{0}(T)_{\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}}}\to 1

∎

This suggest the definition of the following Haar measure.

Construction 3.32
.

Let
d\mu_{T}
be
\frac{1}{\#\pi_{0}(T)(F)}
times the counting measure on
\HH^{1}(F,T)
and
d\mu_{L}
the dual measure via the pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{PT},F}
. In other words
d\mu_{L}
is
\frac{\#\pi_{0}(T_{v})(F_{v})}{\#\HH^{1}(F,T)}
times the counting measure.

If
T
has good reduction then
d\mu_{L}(\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O},T))=1
by Lemma
3.31
.

We can now extend this adelically. Let
F
be a number field,
\mathbf{T}
an adelic group of multiplicative type and
\mathbf{L}
its adelic module of characters.

Construction 3.33
.

We define
d\mu_{\mathbf{T}}:=\prod_{v}d\mu_{T_{v}}
to be the product measure on the restricted product
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})
. This product converges due to Lemma
3.31
. This is a Haar measure as it is a product of Haar measures.

Let
d\mu_{\mathbf{L}}
be the dual Haar measure on
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{L})
via the Poitou-Tate pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{PT}}
. In other words
d\mu_{\mathbf{L}}
is the product measure
\prod_{v}d\mu_{L_{v}}
.

The perfect pairing (
8
) induces a perfect pairing

e^{2\pi i\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{PT}}}:\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})\times\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{L})\to S^{1}:(x,y)\to e^{2\pi i\langle x,y\rangle_{\text{PT}}}

which identifies the Pontryagin dual of
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})
with
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{L})
.

Note that the group
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})/(\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T}))
is compact since it is Pontryagin dual to the discrete group
\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})
by Proposition
3.29
.

Definition 3.34
.

The
Tamagawa number
of
\mathbf{T}
is defined as

\tau(\mathbf{T}):=\frac{\#\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})}{\#\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})}d\mu_{\mathbf{T}}(\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})/(\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})))

where
\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})
is given the counting measure.

Remark 3.35
.

For tori
T
the Tamagawa number is usually defined in terms of the volume of
T(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T(F)
with respect to a certain natural measure, the Tamagawa measure. It was shown by Ono
[
Ono63
]
that with this definition it is equal to
\#\HH^{1}(F,L)/\#\Sha^{1}(F,T)
. We will show in Corollary
3.38
that our definition recovers the same number.

Example 3.36
.

If
T
is a finite group of multiplicative type then
\tau(T)=\frac{\#L^{\Gamma_{F}}}{\#T(F)}
by
[
DY22
, Lemma 3.1.5]
.

The justification for this seemingly strange definition is the following corollary of the Poisson summation formula.

Corollary 3.37
.

Let
f:\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})\to\mathbb{C}
be an
L^{1}
-function and assume that the Poisson summation formula (
4
) holds for
f
. Then

(9)

\tau(\mathbf{T})\sum_{x\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})}f(x)=\sum_{y\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})}\hat{f}(y).

Proof.

The isomorphism
(\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})/(\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T}))^{\vee}\cong\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})
coming from Proposition
3.29
induces a measure on
\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})
. Since
\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})
is discrete this measure is
\mu_{T}(\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})/(\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T}))^{-1}
times the counting measure.

The Poisson summation formula thus takes the following form

\sum_{x\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})}f(x)=\frac{\sum_{y\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})}\hat{f}(y)}{\mu_{\mathbf{T}}(\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})/(\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T}))}.

We can rewrite this equation as

\tau(\mathbf{T})\#\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})\sum_{x\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{T})}f(x)=\#\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})\sum_{y\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})/\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbf{L})}\hat{f}(y)

which is the same as (
9
).
∎

Corollary 3.38
.

Let
T
be a torus over
F
. Then
\tau(T)=\frac{\#\HH^{1}(F,L)}{\#\Sha^{1}(F,T)}
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.31
that the topology on
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},T)
is the discrete topology. Let
f:\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},T)\to\mathbb{C}
be the indicator function of
0
. Then
\hat{f}:\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{L})\to\mathbb{C}
is the constant function
1
. So (
9
) becomes
\tau(T)\#\Sha^{1}(F,T)=\#\HH^{1}(F,L)
.
∎

4.
Metrics on torsors

We will develop the theory of metrics on torsors in this section. Our definition of a metric on a line bundle is slightly different to
[
CLT10a
, §2]
since we will only allow them to take values in
q^{\mathbb{Z}}\cup\{0\}
. But this has essentially no effect on the heights, which is what we are eventually interested in.

4.1.
Local metrics on torsors

For this section let
F
be a local field and let
\mathcal{O}^{\times}\subset F^{\times}
be the subgroup of norm
1
elements. If
F
is non-archimedean let
q
be the size of its residue field. Let
X
be a variety over
F
and
L
a line bundle on
X
. Let
\pi:L\to X
be the total space of
L
and let
L^{\neq 0}:=L-X
where we identify
X
with the zero section
X\subset L
, this is the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor corresponding to
L
.

Definition 4.1
.

A
metric
on
L
is a continuous function
||\cdot||:L(F)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
whose image lies in
q^{\mathbb{Z}}\cup\{0\}
if
F
is non-archimedean and such that for all
x\in X(F)
the restriction of
||\cdot||
to the vector space
L(x)
is a norm.

If
F
is archimedean and
X
is smooth then we say that the metric
||\cdot||
is
smooth
if is smooth as a map
L(F)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

The above data is clearly equivalent to a continuous map
L^{\neq 0}(F)\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}
whose image lies in
q^{\mathbb{Z}}
if
F
is non-archimedean and such that for all
y\in L^{\neq 0}(F)
and
f\in F^{\times}
we have
||f\cdot y||=|f|\cdot||y||
.

Remark 4.2
.

We can write this definition in a more uniform way. Note that
|\cdot|
identifies
F^{\times}/\mathcal{O}^{\times}
with
q^{\mathbb{Z}}
if
F
is non-archimedean and with
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}
if
F
is archimedean. We can thus redefine a metric to be a
F^{\times}
-equivariant map
L^{\neq 0}(F)\to F^{\times}/\mathcal{O}^{\times}
.

Example 4.3
.

Consider the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor
\mathbb{A}^{n+1}_{F}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{P}^{n}_{F}
, where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{n+1}_{F}
by coordinate-wise multiplication. This corresponds to the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(-1)
. A simple example of a metric on this torsor is given by the following formula

(\mathbb{A}^{n+1}_{F}\setminus\{0\})(F)=F^{n+1}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}:(x_{0},\cdots,x_{n})\to\max(|x_{0}|,\cdots,|x_{n}|).

This definition via torsors easily generalizes to torsors under groups of multiplicative type

Definition 4.4
.

Let
T
be a group of multiplicative type. A
metric
||\cdot||
on a
T
-torsor
\pi:Y\to X
is a
T(F)
-equivariant map

||\cdot||:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to T(F)/T(\mathcal{O}).

Torsors in the étale topology are torsors in the analytic topology in the following sense, we will use this fact freely in what follows.

Lemma 4.5
.

The map
\pi:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to X(F)
is a
T(F)
-principal bundle and the induced map
\pi:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})\to X(F)
is a
T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
-principal bundle in the analytic topology.

Proof.

The first statement is proven in
[
Sal98
, p. 127]
. The second follows immediately from the first.
∎

Such torsors always split.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\pi:Y\to X
be any
T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
-principal bundle of
F
-analytic manifolds. Then
\pi
has a continuous section.

Proof.

Let
\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}=X
be an open cover such that we have local sections
\psi_{i}:U_{i}\to Y
. Choose a partition of unity
(\varphi_{i}:X\to\mathbb{R})_{i\in I}
subordinate to this cover, i.e. the support of
\varphi_{i}
is contained in
U_{i}
and
\sum_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}=1
. If
F
is non-archimedean then we may also assume that the image of
\varphi_{i}
is contained in
\mathbb{Z}
.

The group
T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
is an
\mathbb{R}
-vector space if
F
is archimedean so the sum
\sum_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}\psi_{i}
is a well-defined section of
\pi
.
∎

Salberger
[
Sal98
, §9]
observed that one can associate analytic sections to metrics and made this explicit for a certain special metric for toric varieties. This correspondence holds in a much larger generality.

Proposition 4.7
.

There is a bijection between metrics
||\cdot||:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
and continuous sections
\varphi
of the
T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
-torsor
\pi:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})\to X(F)

If the section
\varphi
and metric
||\cdot||
correspond to each other then
||\varphi(x)||=1
for all
x\in X(F)
and
y=||y||\cdot\varphi(\pi(y))
for all
y\in\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

A metric is the same as a trivialization of the
T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
-principal bundle
\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})\to X(F)
so this is just the standard correspondence between trivializations and sections.
∎

Example 4.8
.

Let
F
be a non-archimedean field and
T
be a group of multiplicative type over
F
with good reduction. Let
\pi:Y\to X
be a
T
-torsor over
F
and
\pi:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
an integral model of this torsor. Assume also that
\mathcal{X}
is proper. In this case we have
\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})=X(F)
by the valuative criterion of properness.

The map
\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)}\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})\to\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})
is a
T(\mathcal{O})
-torsor so we have
\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)}\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})/T(\mathcal{O})\cong\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})
. We can define a section as the following composition

X(F)=\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})\cong\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)}\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})/T(\mathcal{O})\subset\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O}).

This section induces a metric
||\cdot||
which is uniquely determined by the property that
||y||=1
if and only if
y\in\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})
for some
a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)
.

If
T=\mathbb{G}_{m}
then this is the metric corresponding to an integral model of a line bundle
[
CLT10a
, §.1.5]
. This suggest the following definition.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
F
be a non-archimedean field and
T
be a group of multiplicative type over
F
with good reduction. Let
\pi:Y\to X
be a
T
-torsor over
F
and
\pi:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
an integral model, which is a
T
-torsor over
\mathcal{O}
.

A metric
||\cdot||:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
is
\mathcal{Y}
-integral
if
||y||=1
for all
a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})
.

A section
\psi:X(F)\to\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
is
\mathcal{Y}
-integral
if the image of
\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})
under
\psi
is equal to
\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)}\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})/T(\mathcal{O})
.

It is clear that a metric is
\mathcal{Y}
-integral if and only if the corresponding section is
\mathcal{Y}
-integral. Note that there can be multiple
\mathcal{Y}
-integral metrics if
\mathcal{X}
is not proper.

Sections are functorial in
T
, which implies that metrics are functorial in
T
too.

Construction 4.10
.

Let
T,T^{\prime}
be groups of multiplicative type,
f:T\to T^{\prime}
a group homomorphism and
\pi:Y\to X
a
T
-torsor. Consider the induced
T^{\prime}
-torsor
Y^{\prime}=T^{\prime}\times^{T}Y
. The
T
-equivariant map
Y\to Y^{\prime}
induces after twisting a
T(F)
-equivariant map coming component-wise from (
1
)

\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F).

Any section
\psi:X(F)\to\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
can be composed with this map to get a section
\psi^{\prime}:X(F)\to\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)/T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
.

Let
||\cdot||:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
and
||\cdot||^{\prime}:\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)\to T^{\prime}(F)/T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
be the metrics corresponding respectively to the sections
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
. It follows directly from the construction that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\prime}(F)/T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces||\cdot||^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces||\cdot||}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

An important special case is when
T^{\prime}=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and we have the character
\chi:T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The above construction then produces a section and a metric on a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
torsor. This allows us to think of a metric on a
T
-torsor as a collection of metrics on line bundles, one for each character of
T
. This is similar to the notion of a system of metrics
[
Pey98
, §4]
, but metrics on torsors are more natural.

The above construction also behaves well with respect to integrality.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
F
be non-archimedean and
T,T^{\prime}
groups of multiplicative type with good reduction. Let
\pi:Y\to X
a
T
-torsor with integral model
\pi:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
. The induced
T^{\prime}
-torsor
Y^{\prime}=T^{\prime}\times_{T}Y
has an induced integral model
\mathcal{Y}:=T^{\prime}\times_{T}\mathcal{Y}
.

If a section
\psi:X(F)\to\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
is
\mathcal{Y}
-integral then the section
\psi^{\prime}
of Construction
4.10
is
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
-integral.

Proof.

Follows immediately from the definitions and the fact that (
1
) is functorial in
S
.
∎

Let us also note that we may always assume that if
T\to T^{\prime}
is surjective then any metric on a
T^{\prime}
-torsor comes from a
T
-torsor.

Lemma 4.12
.

In the situation of Construction
4.10
assume that
f:T\to T^{\prime}
is surjective. Let
||\cdot||^{\prime}:\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)\to T^{\prime}(F)/T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
be a metric. Then there exists a metric
||\cdot||:\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to T(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
such that
||\cdot||^{\prime}
is induced by
||\cdot||
via Construction
4.10
.

Moreover, if
T
and
T^{\prime}
have good reduction and
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
is an integral model with induced integral model
\mathcal{Y^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}
such that
||\cdot||^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
-integral then we may take
||\cdot||
to be
\mathcal{Y}
-integral.

Proof.

Let
G:=\ker(T\to T^{\prime})
. We claim that for each
a\in\HH^{1}(F,T)
the map
Y_{a}\to Y_{a^{\prime}}
is a
G
-torsor. This can be checked étale locally on
X
so we may assume that
Y_{a}=T\times X
and
Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}=T^{\prime}\times Y
. The map
T\to T^{\prime}
is a
G
-torsor since it is surjective so the claim follows.

The map
\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)\to\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)
is thus a
G(F)
-principal bundle so

\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})\to\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)/T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})

is a
G(F)/\ker(T(\mathcal{O})\to T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}))=G(F)/G(\mathcal{O})
-principal bundle. By Lemma
4.6
this map has a section
\sigma
.

By composing
\sigma
with the section
\psi^{\prime}:X(F)\to\coprod_{a^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F,T^{\prime})}Y^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}(F)/T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
corresponding to
||\cdot||^{\prime}
we construct a section
\psi:X(F)\to\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
which corresponds to a metric with the desired properties.

If the metric
||\cdot||^{\prime}
is integral then we may take
\psi
such that when restricted to the clopen subset
\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})
it is given by
\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O})\cong\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)}\mathcal{Y}_{a}(\mathcal{O})/T(\mathcal{O})\subset\coprod_{a\in H^{1}(F,T)}Y_{a}(F)/T(\mathcal{O})
.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

Applying this to
T^{\prime}=1
we deduce that any torsor has a metric.

4.2.
Adelic metrics and heights

The story in the previous section can also be done adelically. For this section we let
F
be a number field,
\mathbf{T}
an adelic group of multiplicative type over
F
,
\mathbf{X}
an adelic variety over
F
and
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
a
\mathbf{T}
-torsor.

Definition 4.14
.

An
adelic metric
on
\bm{\pi}
is a collection
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
where
||\cdot||_{v}
is a metric on
\pi_{v}:Y_{v}\to X_{v}
such that there exists an integral model
\pi:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
of
\pi_{F}:Y_{F}\to X_{F}
such that
||\cdot||_{v}
is
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}
-integral for all but finitely many places
v
.

Such metrics are functorial in
T
.

Construction 4.15
.

Let
\mathbf{f}:\mathbf{T}\to\mathbf{T}^{\prime}
be a map of adelic groups of multiplicative type. We can consider the
adelic contracted product

\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{Y}\times^{\mathbf{T}}\mathbf{T^{\prime}}:=(Y_{F}\times_{T_{F}}T^{\prime}_{F},(Y_{v}\times_{T_{v}}T^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}},(\xi_{Y,v}\times_{T_{v}}T^{\prime}_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}).

Applying Construction
4.10
to every local metric
||\cdot||_{v}
for each
v
provides a local metric
||\cdot||_{v}
on the torsor
Y_{L,v}
for all
v\in\Omega_{F}
. It follows immediately from Lemma
4.11
and the definitions that this collection forms an adelic metric on
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

Given an adelic metric we can define a total metric and a height function. In what follows we will slightly abuse notation and use
\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
to denote the restricted product of
\coprod_{a_{v}\in H^{1}(F_{v},T_{v})}Y_{v,a_{v}}(F_{v})
for all
v\in\Omega_{F}
with respect to the compact subspaces
\coprod_{a_{v}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T_{v})}\mathcal{Y}_{a_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
for
v\not\in S
and any
\mathcal{O}_{F,S}
-integral model
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
.

Construction 4.16
.

Let
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
be an adelic metric. Taking their restricted product we get the
total metric

||\cdot||:=\prod_{v}||\cdot||_{v}:\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{O}}).

We define the
adelic height
corresponding to
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
as the composition

\mathbf{H}:=|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}}^{-1}\circ||\cdot||:\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Hom(\hat{\mathbf{T}},\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}).

Given
L\in\hat{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathbb{C}}
we denote by
\mathbf{H}(\cdot)^{L}
the evaluation of
\mathbf{H}
at
L
, i.e.
|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}}^{-L}\circ||\cdot||
.

The map
||\cdot||
is well-defined due to the existence of an integral model in the definition of adelic metric.

Both
||\cdot||
and
\mathbf{H}
are
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-equivariant, with
t\in\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
acting by
|t|_{\mathbf{T}}^{-1}
on
\Hom(\hat{\mathbf{T}},\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times})
, by construction.

If
x\in X_{F}(F)
then there exists a unique
a\in H^{1}(F,T_{F})
and a point
y\in Y_{a}(F)
such that
\pi_{a}(y)=x
. The point
y
is moreover unique up to multiplication by
t\in T(F)
. But
|t|_{T}=1
by the product formula so
\mathbf{H}(y)
is independent of the choice of
y
. The following is thus well-defined.

Definition 4.17
.

Given
x\in X_{F}(F)
we define the
height
of
x
with respect to the adelic metric
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
as
\mathbf{H}(x):=\mathbf{H}(y)
for any choice of
y\in Y_{a}(F)
such that
\pi_{a}(y)=x
.

Unfolding the definitions we see that if
T=\mathbb{G}_{m}
then this is equivalent to the usual definition of the height in terms of adelically metrized line bundles.

Remark 4.18
.

Instead of defining the height function by an adelic metric one could define an adelic height function on the torsor
\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
to be any
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-equivariant map
\mathbf{H}:\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Hom(\hat{\mathbf{T}},\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times})
.

Given any character
\chi\in\hat{\mathbf{T}}
we can apply Construction
4.15
to get an adelic metric on a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor, which thus gives a height function coming from an adelically metrized line bundle. It follows directly from the constructions that this height is equal to specializing
\mathbf{H}
at
\chi
.

The height has the following positivity property.

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
\mathbf{D}\subset\mathbf{X}
be an effective divisor on an adelic variety
\mathbf{X}
. Let
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
be the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor corresponding to the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{D})
.

Let
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
be an adelic metric on the torsor
\bm{\pi}
and
C\subset\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
a compact subset. There exists a constant
\delta>0
such that for all
x\in(X_{F}\setminus D_{F})(F)\cap C
we have
\mathbf{H}(x)>\delta\in\Hom(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times})=\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}
.

Proof.

For each place
v
let
1_{D_{v}}\in\Gamma(X_{v},\mathcal{O}(D_{v}))
be the canonical section defining
D_{v}
. This defines a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
f_{v}:Y_{v}\to\mathbb{A}_{F_{v}}^{1}
, where
\lambda\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
act by multiplication by
\lambda^{-1}
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. These maps fit together into a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map of adelic varieties
\mathbf{f}:\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbb{A}_{F}^{1}
. It follows from the construction that
\mathbf{f}^{-1}(0)=\bm{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{D})
.

Let
x\in(X_{F}\setminus D_{F})(F)\cap C
and
y\in Y(F)
such that
\pi(y)=x
. We have the equality
y=\mathbf{H}(y)^{-1}\psi(x)\mod\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
and since
\mathbf{f}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant this implies the equality
\mathbf{f}(y)=\mathbf{H}(y)\mathbf{f}(\psi(x))
in
\mathbb{A}_{F}/\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
. Since
x\not\in D_{F}(F)
we know that
\mathbf{f}(y)\neq 0
. Taking the norm we deduce the following equality from the product formula

1=|\mathbf{f}(y)|=\mathbf{H}(x)|\mathbf{f}(\psi(x))|.

The composition

\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{f}}\mathbb{A}_{F}/\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\xrightarrow{|\cdot|}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

is continuous so the image of
C
is bounded from above, this implies that
|\mathbf{f}(\psi(x))|
is bounded from above and thus that
\mathbf{H}(x)
is bounded from below.
∎

Corollary 4.20
.

Let
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
be an adelic metric on the
\mathbf{T}
-torsor
\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
and let
\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{n}\in\hat{\mathbf{T}}
be characters whose corresponding line bundles correspond to effective divisors
D_{1},\cdots,D_{n}
. Let
\Lambda=\{\lambda\in\hat{T}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}:c(\chi_{i})\geq 0\emph{forall}i\}
be the dual cone to the
\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{n}
and assume that it is full-dimensional.

For any compact subset
C\subset\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
a compact subset There exists an
\ell\in\hat{T}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that for all
x\in(X_{F}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{n}D_{i})(F)\cap C
we have
\log\mathbf{H}(x)\in\ell+\Lambda
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
4.19
to the adelic metric induced on the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
torsor corresponding to
\chi_{i}
for all
i
to find a constant
\delta_{i}>0
. We thus have that
\mathbf{H}(x)(\chi_{i})>\delta_{i}
for all
i
.

It thus suffices to choose
\ell\in\hat{T}^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\ell(\chi_{i})\leq\log\delta_{i}
for all
i
. Such an
\ell
exists because
\Lambda
is full-dimensional.
∎

4.3.
Tamagawa measures

In
[
CLT10a
, §2.1, §2.4]
Tamagawa measures are associated to metrics on line bundles. For the sake of clarity we rewrite all the relevant definitions in our language.

4.3.1.
Local residue measures

Let
F
be a local field,
X
a smooth geometrically integral variety and
D\subset X
a divisor with geometrically simple normal crossings with geometric components
D_{1},D_{2},\cdots,D_{n}\subset X_{F^{\sep}}
. Let
Z:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}
, it is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant and thus defines a closed subvariety of
X
.

Let
\omega_{X}(D)
be the sheaf of top degree differential forms with at most a single pole along
D
and
\omega_{X}(D)^{\neq 0}\to X
the corresponding
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor. Let
||\cdot||:\omega_{X}(D)^{\neq 0}(F)\to\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}
be a metric.

Repeated applications of the adjunction formula, see
[
CLT10a
, §2.1.12]
, defines an isomorphism
\omega_{X_{F^{\sep}}(D)}|_{Z^{F^{\sep}}}\cong\omega_{Z_{F^{\sep}}}
which is canonical up to a sign. If
D_{i}
is defined by the local equation
f_{i}
then the inverse is explicitly given by lifting an
m:=\dim Z
-form

\omega_{Z}
on
Z
to an
m
-form
\tilde{\omega}
on
X
and sending

\omega_{Z}\to\omega_{X}=\tilde{\omega}\wedge\frac{df_{1}}{f_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{df_{n}}{f_{n}}.

Assume that
\omega_{Z}
is defined over
F
. Then
\Gamma_{F}
acts via a character
\Gamma_{F}\to\mu_{2}
on
\omega_{X}
. There exists an
a\in F^{\sep,\times}
such that
a\omega_{X}
is defined over
F
by Hilbert theorem 90.

On each open
V\subset Z(F)
on which there exists a non-vanishing differential form
\omega_{Z}
, i.e. a section
V\to\omega_{Z}^{\neq 0}(F)
we define a local residue measure on
U
by the formula
\tau_{||\cdot||}:=|a|\frac{|\omega_{Z}|}{||a\omega_{X}||}
, where
|\omega_{Z}|
is defined in the usual way, e.g.
[
CLT10a
, §2.1.7]
. This measure is independent of the choice of
\omega_{Z}
and
a
since if
g:V\to F^{\times}
and
\lambda\in F^{\times}
then
|\lambda a|\frac{|g\omega_{Z}|}{||\lambda ag\omega_{X}||}=|a|\frac{|\omega_{Z}|}{||a\omega_{X}||}
.

These measures can thus be glued to a measure
\tau_{||\cdot||}
on
Z(F)
. We will also denote the pushforward measure along the closed immersion
Z(F)\subset X(F)
by
\tau_{||\cdot||}
.

4.3.2.
Global measures

Let
F
be a number field,
X
a smooth proper geometrically integral variety and
D\subset X
a divisor with geometrically simple normal crossings and
U:=X\setminus D
. Let
\mathbf{A}=((A_{v},Z_{v}))_{v}\in\mathcal{C}_{\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}^{\an}(D)
be a face.

For each place
v
the divisor
D_{A_{v}}=\sum_{\alpha_{v}\in A_{v}}D_{\alpha_{v}}
is defined over
F_{v}
and
(X;A_{v},Z_{v})\setminus D_{A_{v}}=U_{F_{v}}
. We have an equality
\omega_{(X;A_{v},Z_{v})}(D_{A_{v}})|_{U_{F_{v}}}=\omega_{U_{F_{v}}}
so the tuple
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}:=(\omega_{U}^{\neq 0},(\omega_{(X;A_{v},Z_{v})}(D_{A_{v}})^{\neq 0})_{v\in\Omega_{F}})
forms an adelic variety. The map
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}\to(X;\mathbf{A})
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor.

Let
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v}
be an adelic metric on the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}\to(X;\mathbf{A})
. Applying the construction of §
4.3.1
for each place
v
defines a local Tamagawa measure
\tau_{||\cdot||_{v}}
on
Z_{v}^{\circ}(F_{v})
.

Definition 4.21
.

If
\HH^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=\HH^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
then the
Tamagawa measure

\tau_{\mathbf{H}}
on
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}}Z_{v}^{\circ}(F_{v})
is the product measure

c_{\mathbf{A}}L^{*}(1,\Pic U_{\overline{F}})^{-1}L^{*}(1,\overline{F}[U]^{\times}/\overline{F}^{\times})\prod_{v}L_{v}(1,\Pic U_{\overline{F}})L^{*}(1,\overline{F}[U]^{\times}/\overline{F}^{\times})^{-1}\tau_{||\cdot||_{v}}.

where

(10)

c_{\mathbf{A}}:=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}2^{\#\{\alpha_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{\alpha_{v}}=\mathbb{R}\}}(2\pi)^{\#\{\alpha_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{\alpha_{v}}=\mathbb{C}\}}.

The pushforward of this measure along the closed inclusion
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
will also be denoted by
\tau_{\mathbf{H}}
.

This product measure is well-defined by
[
CLT10a
, Thm. 2.5]
.

Remark 4.22
.

The powers of
2
and
2\pi
are introduced in
[
CLT10b
, p. 10]
. The authors also provide a definition in the case that
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{C}_{S}^{\an}(D)
for
S\neq\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
, but it is unclear to us if the
\log q_{v}
should appear here or in the cone constant for Manin’s conjecture.

The Tamagawa measure only depends on the height
\mathbf{H}
associated to the adelic metric
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v}
. This explains our choice of notation
\tau_{\mathbf{H}}
.

Lemma 4.23
.

Let
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v}
and
(||\cdot||^{\prime}_{v})_{v}
be two adelic metrics on
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}\to(X;\mathbf{A})
with corresponding height functions
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{H^{\prime}}
respectively. The quotient
\mathbf{H}/\mathbf{H}^{\prime}
descends a continuous function
X(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Hom(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0})=\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}
and
\tau_{\mathbf{H}}=\mathbf{H}/\mathbf{H}^{\prime}\tau_{\mathbf{H}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

The statement about descent follows since
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
is a principal
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-bundle and
\mathbf{H}/\mathbf{H}^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-invariant.

There exist continuous functions
g_{v}:(X;A_{v},Z_{v})(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
for each
v
such that
||\cdot||_{v}=(g_{v}\circ\pi_{v})\cdot||\cdot||_{v}^{\prime}
for all places
v
. By definition we then have
(g_{v}\circ\pi_{v})\cdot\tau_{||\cdot||_{v}}=(g_{v}\circ\pi_{v})\tau_{||\cdot||^{\prime}_{v}}
.

Taking products we get
\prod_{v}(g_{v}\circ\pi_{v})\cdot\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{H}^{\prime}
and
\prod_{v}(g_{v}\circ\pi_{v})\tau_{\mathbf{H}}=\tau_{\mathbf{H}^{\prime}}
.
∎

Remark 4.24
.

This lemma allows one to define Tamagawa measures for height functions which do not come from adelic metrics. If
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v}
is an adelic metric with corresponding height function
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{H^{\prime}}
is another height function then we put

\tau_{\mathbf{H}^{\prime}}:=\mathbf{H}/\mathbf{H}^{\prime}\tau_{\mathbf{H}}
.

4.3.3.
Tamagawa measures on adelic groups of multiplicative type

A torus
T
has a canonical metric on
\omega_{T}

[
Sal98
, Def. 3.29]
. The definition easily extends to groups of multiplicative type.

Construction 4.25
.

Let
T
be a group of multiplicative type over any field
F
of rank
n
. There is a map
X^{*}(T)\to\HH^{0}(T_{F^{\sep}},\Omega_{T_{F^{\sep}}/F^{\sep}}^{1})
sending a character
\chi:T_{F^{\sep}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
to the
T
-invariant differential
d\chi/\chi
. Note that torsion characters are locally constant and are thus mapped to
0
.

This map is clearly
\Gamma_{F}
-equivariant and thus descents to a map
X^{*}(T)\to\Omega_{T_{F}/F}^{1}
of étale sheaves on
T
. Taking wedge products we get a map
\wedge^{n}X^{*}(T)\to\wedge^{n}\Omega_{T}^{1}=\omega_{T}
. If
n=0
then this is the map
\wedge^{0}X^{*}(T)=\mathbb{Z}\to\mathcal{O}_{T}=\wedge^{0}\Omega_{T}^{1}
sending
1
to
1
.

The group
X^{*}(T)
has rank
n
so
\wedge^{n}X^{*}(T)
modulo torsion is
\mathbb{Z}
is with a potentially non-trivial
\Gamma_{F}
-action. One checks that the image of a generator of
\mathbb{Z}
is a nowhere vanishing differential form, i.e. is an element of
\HH^{0}(T,\omega_{T_{F^{\sep}}}^{\neq 0})
. We denote it by
d\log T
, it is unique up to multiplication by
-1
.

If
F
is a local field then extend the absolute value
|\cdot|_{F}
to
F^{\sep}
. For any
T
-invariant nowhere vanishing differential form
\omega\in\HH^{0}(T,\omega_{T_{F^{\sep}}}^{\neq 0})
let
a\in F^{\sep}
be such that
a\cdot\omega=d\log T
. Define the measure
\tau_{T}=|a|_{F}|\omega|
on
T(F_{v})
. The fact that
\omega
is
T
-invariant implies that
\tau_{T}
is a Haar measure.

If
T
has good reduction then the above constructions can all be done over
\mathcal{O}
. So if
\omega
is defined over
\mathcal{O}
then we may take
a\in F^{\sep}
with
|a|=1
.

This construction has the following compatibility with exact sequences.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
1\to T^{\prime}\to T\to T^{\prime\prime}\to 1
be an exact sequence of groups of multiplicative type over a local field. The measure
\tau_{T}^{\prime\prime}
restricted to the image of
T(F)
is equal to the quotient measure
\tau_{T}/\tau_{T^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Because
T
is an algebraic group we can identify
\omega_{T}
with its fiber at the identity. Under this identification we have an isomorphism
\omega_{T}\cong\omega_{T^{\prime}}\otimes\omega_{T^{\prime\prime}}
and it is clear by construction that
d\log_{T}=d\log_{T^{\prime}}d\log_{T^{\prime\prime}}
under this isomorphism. The lemma follows.
∎

To get an adelic Tamagawa measure on
T(F)
we will still have to multiply by convergence factors. These will be determined by the following lemma

Lemma 4.27
.

Let
T
be a group of multiplicative type of dimension
n
over a non-archimedean local field
F
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}
. Assume that
T
has good reduction. Then we have
\tau_{T}(T(\mathcal{O}))=\#\pi_{0}(T)(F)L_{F}(1,X^{*}(T))^{-1}\Disc_{F}^{-\frac{n}{2}}
.

Proof.

Let
T^{\prime}\subset T
be the connected component of the identity. This is a torus which has good reduction and
X^{*}(T^{\prime})_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong X^{*}(T)_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

We know by Lemma
3.31
that
\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O},T^{\prime})=0
. The exact sequence
1\to T^{\prime}\to T\to\pi_{0}(T)\to 1
thus induces an exact sequence
1\to T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})\to T(\mathcal{O})\to\pi_{0}(T)(\mathcal{O})=\pi_{0}(T)(F)\to 1
. We have
\tau_{T}(T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}))=\tau_{T^{\prime}}(T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}))=\Disc_{F}^{-\frac{n}{2}}L_{F}(1,X^{*}(T))^{-1}
by
[
Ono61
, §3.3]

3
3

3

The presence of
\Disc_{F}^{-\frac{n}{2}}
is due to our choice of Haar measure on
F
.
The lemma follows.
∎

This lemma implies that the following product measure exists.

Definition 4.28
.

Let
\mathbf{T}
be an adelic group of multiplicative type over a global field. The
global Tamagawa measure
is defined as the product measure
\tau_{\mathbf{T}}:=L^{*}(1,X^{*}(T_{F}))^{-1}\prod_{v}(\#\pi_{0}(T)(F_{v}))^{-1}L_{v}(1,X^{*}(T_{F}))\tau_{T_{v}}
on
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
.

Definition 4.29
.

Assume that
T_{F}\to T_{v}
is an isomorphism for all non-archimedean
v
. Then by Lemma
3.27
there exists an exact sequence

0\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\xrightarrow{\log|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}}}\hat{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}\to 0

Let
dx_{\hat{\mathbf{T}}}
be the Haar measure on
\hat{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}
. We let
\tau_{\mathbf{T}^{1}}
be the Haar measure on
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
such that
\tau_{\mathbf{T}}=\tau_{\mathbf{T}^{1}}dx_{\hat{\mathbf{T}}}
.

The adelic groups of multiplicative type we will encounter will have a particular form which will allow us to compute the volume
\tau_{\mathbf{T}}^{1}(\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{F}(F))
.

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
\mathbf{T}
be an adelic group of multiplicative type. Assume that there exists a torus
T^{\prime}
over
F
and a finite
\Gamma_{F}
-set
E
such that there exists an exact sequence

(11)

1\to T_{F}\to T^{\prime}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}\to 1

Assume moreover that for each place
v
there exists a finite
\Gamma_{v}
-set
A_{v}
such that
A_{v}=\emptyset
when
v
is non-archimedean and for each place
v
there exist a short exact sequence of groups of multiplicative type

1\to T_{v}\to(T^{\prime})_{F_{v}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}\to 1

which is compatible with the sequence (
11
).

We then have

\tau_{\mathbf{T}^{1}}\left(\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T(F)\right)=\tau(\mathbf{T})\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}2^{\#\{a_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{a_{v}}=\mathbb{R}\}}(2\pi)^{\#\{a_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{a_{v}}=\mathbb{C}\}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.7
one has
\HH^{1}(F,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E})=\HH^{1}(F_{v},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E})=0
and
\HH^{1}(F_{v},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}})=0
for all places
v
.

We thus have a long exact sequence

(12)

1\to T_{F}(F)\to T^{\prime}(F)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(F)\to\HH^{1}(F,T_{F})\to\HH^{1}(F,T^{\prime})\to 0

and for each place
v
we get a long exact sequence

(13)

1\to T_{v}(F_{v})\to T^{\prime}(F_{v})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(F_{v})\to\HH^{1}(F,T_{v})\to\HH^{1}(F,T^{\prime})\to 0

Moreover, if
v
is a place of good reduction for all the relevant groups of multiplicative type we have an exact sequence

(14)

1\to T_{v}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to T^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O},T_{v})\to\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T^{\prime})\to 0

The exact sequences (
13
) and (
14
) are compatible with the local Tamagawa measures by Lemma
4.26
, where we consider the cohomology groups to be equipped with the counting measure.

Taking restricted products of (
13
) we deduce that the following sequence is exact

(15)

1\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to T^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(\mathbb{A}_{v})\to\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})\to\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},T^{\prime})\to 0

This sequence is compatible with the global Tamagawa measures, where we equip the cohomology groups with the measures of Construction
3.33
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E_{v}}(F_{v})
with the local Tamagawa measure. Indeed, the convergence factors are compatible since
\#\pi_{0}(T_{v})
appears both in the measure on
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
and the measure on
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})
and
L_{v}(1,X^{*}(T_{F}))\cdot L_{v}(1,\mathbb{Z}[E])=L_{v}(1,X^{*}(T^{\prime}))
for all places
v
and
L^{*}(1,X^{*}(T_{F}))\cdot L^{*}(1,\mathbb{Z}[E])=L^{*}(1,X^{*}(T^{\prime}))
.

Dually, Lemma
2.7
implies that
\HH^{1}(F,\mathbb{Z}[A])=0
,
\HH^{1}(F_{v},\mathbb{Z}[A_{v}])=0
and
\HH^{1}(F_{v},\mathbb{Z}[E_{v}])=0
for all places
v
so we have a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}[E/\Gamma_{F}]\to\hat{T^{\prime}}\to\hat{T_{F}}\to 0

and for each place
v
a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}[E/\Gamma_{v}]\to\hat{T}^{\prime}_{F_{v}}\times\mathbb{Z}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]\to\hat{T_{v}}\to 0

It follows from the definition of
\hat{\mathbf{T}}
that there is a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}[E/\Gamma_{v}]\to\hat{T}^{\prime}_{F_{v}}\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{Z}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]\to\hat{\mathbf{T}}\to 0

By Lemma
2.14
we get the following short exact sequence of
\mathbb{R}
-vector spaces equipped with compatible Haar measures

(16)

0\to\hat{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}\to\hat{T}^{{}^{\prime}*}_{F_{v},\mathbb{R}}\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{R}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]\to\mathbb{R}[E/\Gamma_{v}]\to 0

The logarithmic norm maps the exact sequence (
15
) to (
16
) and these maps are surjective by Lemma
3.27
. Taking kernels we thus get an exact sequence

1\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\to T^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(\mathbb{A}_{v})^{1}\to\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T})\to\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},T^{\prime})\to 0

The relevant Tamagawa measures are again compatible.

Qoutienting this by the exact sequence (
12
) we get an exact sequence of compact groups with compatible measures. This implies the equality

\begin{split}&\tau_{\mathbf{T}^{1}}\left(\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T(F)\right)\tau_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A,1}}\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(F)\right)\frac{\tau(T^{\prime})}{\#\Sha^{1}(F,X^{*}(T^{\prime}))}=\\
&\tau_{T^{\prime 1}}\left(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T^{\prime}(F)\right)\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\tau_{v}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v}))\frac{\tau(\mathbf{T})}{\#\Sha^{1}(F,X^{*}(\mathbf{T}))}.\end{split}

Now
\tau_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E,1}}\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(F)\right)=1
by
[
Ono61
, Thm. 3.5.1]
and
\tau_{T^{\prime 1}}\left(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T^{\prime}(F)\right)=\tau(T^{\prime})
by
[
Ono63
, Main Thm.]
and Corollary
3.38
.

Since
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
is a product of complex unit circles
S^{1}
and the group
\{1,-1\}
, respectively equipped with the Lebesgue measure and the counting measure it only remains to show that
\#\Sha^{1}(F,X^{*}(T^{\prime}))=\#\Sha^{1}(F,X^{*}(\mathbf{T}))
.

To do this we use that
\HH^{1}(F,\mathbb{Z}[E])=\HH^{1}(F_{v},\mathbb{Z}[E])=\HH^{1}(F_{v},\mathbb{Z}[A_{v}])=0
for all
v
to deduce the exactness of the following sequences

0\to\HH^{1}(F,X^{*}(T^{\prime}))\to\HH^{1}(F,X^{*}(T_{F}))\to\HH^{2}(F,\mathbb{Z}[E])

0\to\HH^{1}(F_{v},X^{*}(T^{\prime}))\to\HH^{1}(F_{v},X^{*}(T_{v}))\to\HH^{2}(F_{v},\mathbb{Z}[E])

Homological algebra shows that the sequence
0\to\Sha^{1}(F,X^{*}(T^{\prime}))\to\Sha^{1}(F,X^{*}(\mathbf{T}))\to\Sha^{2}(F,\mathbb{Z}[E])
is exact, but
\Sha^{2}(F,\mathbb{Z}[E])
is dual to
\Sha^{1}(F,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E})\subset\HH^{1}(F,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E})=0
by
[
NSW08
, Thm. 8.6.7]
. The proposition follows.
∎

5.
Universal torsors and the Brauer-Manin obstruction

5.1.
The Brauer-Manin pairing

We refer to
[
CTS21
, §12]
for the usual Brauer-Manin pairing for varieties. We will generalize this to adelic varieties. For this section fix a number field
F
and a geometrically integral adelic variety
\mathbf{X}
over
F
. Recall that for each place
v\in\Omega_{F}
there exists an injection
\text{inv}_{v}:\Br F_{v}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
which is surjective if
v
is finite

Definition 5.1
.

The
Brauer-Manin pairing
is defined as the pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{BM}}:\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times\Br\mathbf{X}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}:((x_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}},(A_{F},(A_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}})\to\sum_{v\in\Omega_{F}}\text{inv}_{v}(A_{v}(x_{v})).

It is linear in the second variable. It is well-defined and continuous for the usual reason
[
CTS21
, Prop. 12.3.1]
.

The constant Brauer elements
\Br_{0}\mathbf{X}
are contained in the right kernel of this pairing due to
[
CTS21
, Thm. 12.1.8]
, we thus get an induced pairing
\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times\Br\mathbf{X}/\Br_{0}\mathbf{X}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
, which we will also call the Brauer-Manin pairing.

The
Brauer-Manin set

\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br}
is the left kernel of this pairing. By the usual argument
X_{F}(F)\subset\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br}
.
More generally, for any subset
B\subset\Br\mathbf{X}
or
B\subset\Br\mathbf{X}/\Br_{0}\mathbf{X}
we denote by
\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{B}
the elements of
\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
orthogonal to
B
with respect to the Brauer-Manin pairing. If
B=\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}
then we write
\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br_{1}}:=\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}}
and call it the algebraic Brauer-Manin set.

5.1.1.
Strong approximation

Let
X
be a geometrically integral smooth proper variety over
F
,
D\subset X
a geometrically simple normal crossing divisor. It is known that integral points on
U:=X\setminus D
tend to concentrate near maximal faces of the Clemens complex. The naive form of strong approximation, that
U(F)
is dense in

U(\mathbb{A}_{F})
, will thus often fail, the simplest example is is
U=\mathbb{A}^{1}
. We propose the following alternative definition

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}(D)
be a face of the Clemens complex. We say that
(X;\mathbf{A})
satisfies
strong approximation
if the closure of
U(F)
in
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
contains
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}
.

The Brauer-Manin set
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br}
is closed since the Brauer-Manin pairing is continuous and
U(F)\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br}
. So if
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br}:=U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}\cap(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br}\neq U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}
then
(X;\mathbf{A})
cannot satisfy strong approximation, in this case we will that that there is a
Brauer-Manin obstruction to strong approximation
. If the closure of
U(F)
in
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
contains
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br}
then we say that the Brauer-Manin obstruction is the
only obstruction to strong approximation
. If the closure of
U(F)
in
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
contains
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br_{1}}
then we say that the
algebraic
Brauer-Manin obstruction is the only obstruction to strong approximation.

5.2.
Universal torsors

Let us recall the definition of type for any field
F
.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
T
be a group of multiplicative type and
\pi:Y\to X
a
T
-torsor. Its
type
is the
\Gamma_{F}
-equivariant morphism
\texttt{type}(\pi):X^{*}(T)\to\Pic X_{F^{\sep}}
which sends a character
\chi:T_{F^{\sep}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
to the image of the class
[Y]\in H^{1}(X_{F^{\sep}},T)
under the map
\chi_{*}:H^{1}(X_{F^{\sep}},T)\to H^{1}(X_{F^{\sep}},\mathbb{G}_{m})=\Pic X_{F^{\sep}}
.

There are other equivalent definitions of the type, see
[
Sko01
, Lemma 2.3.1]
.

We can extend this definition to adelic scheme. Let
F
be a number field,
\mathbf{X}
an adelic scheme over
F
and
\mathbf{T}
an adelic group of multiplicative type over
F
. The following is well-defined since the type is functorial and invariant under base change of field.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
be a
\mathbf{T}
-torsor. Its
type
is the morphism of adelic modules

\texttt{type}(\bm{\pi}):X^{*}(\mathbf{T})\to\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X}
given by the tuple
(\texttt{type}(\pi_{F}),(\texttt{type}(\pi_{v}))_{v\in\Omega_{F}})
.

If a variety has a rational point then torsors of every possible type exist. This also holds for adelic varieties.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\mathbf{X}
be an adelic scheme,
\mathbf{L}
a finitely generated adelic module
\mathbf{T}:=D(\mathbf{L})
the Cartier dual and
\bm{\lambda}:\mathbf{L}\to\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X}
a morphism of adelic modules.

If
X_{F}(F)\neq\emptyset
then there exists a torsor of type
\lambda
.

Proof.

Let
x\in X_{F}(F)
. By
[
CTS87
, p. 426]
there exist a
T_{F}
torsor
\pi_{F}:Y_{F}\to X_{F}
of type
\lambda_{F}
such that
\pi_{F}^{-1}(x)\cong T_{F}
which is unique up to (non-unique) isomorphism.

Similarly, for every place
v
there exists a
T_{v}
-torsor
\pi_{v}:Y_{v}\to X_{v}
of type
\lambda_{v}
such that
\pi_{v}^{-1}(\xi_{v}(x))\cong T_{v}
which is unique up to isomorphism.

It follows from the uniqueness up to isomorphism that
(Y_{F})_{F_{v}}\times^{T}T_{v}\cong Y_{v}\times_{X_{v}}(X_{F})_{F_{v}}
for all
v
. We can thus form an adelic variety
\mathbf{Y}:=(Y_{F},(Y_{v})_{v})
and
\bm{\pi}:=(\pi_{F},(\pi_{v})_{v})
is a
\mathbf{T}
-torsor of type
\bm{\lambda}

∎

We can now define the notion of
universal torsors
, which will play a crucial role in our argument.

Definition 5.6
.

Assume that the adelic module
\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X}
is finitely generated as an abelian group
4
4

4

One could remove this assumption by replacing the geometric Picard group
\Pic X_{F^{\sep}}
by the geometric Néron–Severi group.
. The
Néron–Severi torus
5
5

5

We warn that this is not necessarily a torus.
of
X
is the adelic group of multiplicative type
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}:=D(\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X})
.

A
universal torsor
of
X
is a
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}
-torsor
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
whose type
\texttt{type}(\bm{\pi})
is the identity map
\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X}\to\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X}
. In other words,
Y_{F}\to X_{F}
and
Y_{v}\to X_{v}
are universal torsors for all places
v
.

Remark 5.7
.

Universal torsors are not unique, but they are unique up to twists, see
[
Sko01
, (2.22)]
.

One can recover the universal torsor on an open subvariety from the universal torsors of the original variety. The following lemma and construction are probably known, but we were unable to find a reference.

Construction 5.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth variety over a field
K
such that
\Pic X_{K^{\sep}}
is finitely generated and torsion-free and
K^{\sep}[X]^{\times}=K^{\sep,\times}
. Let
T_{\NS X}
be the Néron-Severi torus of
X
and
\pi:Y\to X
a universal torsor. Let
D\subset X
be a divisor.

Let
A
be the
\Gamma_{F}
-set of irreducible components of the divisor
D_{K^{\sep}}\subset X_{K^{\sep}}
, for
a\in A
let
D_{a}
be the corresponding irreducible component. The pullback of
D_{a}
along
Y_{K^{\sep}}
is a principal divisor by
[
CTS87
, Prop. 2.1.1]
so defines a map
Y_{K^{\sep}}\to\mathbb{A}_{K^{\sep}}^{1}
such that the fiber at
0
is
\pi^{-1}(D_{a})
. This map is unique up to multiplication by a constant since by loc. cit.
K^{\sep}[Y]^{\times}=K^{\sep}
.

Multiplying these maps for all
a\in A
defines a map
Y_{F^{\sep}}\to\mathbb{A}_{K^{\sep}}^{A}
, unique up to multiplication by an element of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K^{\sep})
. The obstruction to this map being
\Gamma_{K}
-invariant is thus given by a cocycle
\Gamma_{K}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K^{\sep})
. But
\HH^{1}(K,\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K^{\sep}))=0
by Lemma
2.7
so after multiplication by an element of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K^{\sep})
the map becomes
\Gamma_{K}
-invariant and thus descends to a map
f_{D}:Y\to\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
, unique up to multiplication by
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K)
.

Let
U:=X\setminus D
. Assume that
K^{\sep}[U]^{\times}=K^{\sep,\times}
and let
T_{\NS U}
be the Néron-Severi torus of
U
. This implies that the map of
\Gamma_{K}
-modules
\mathbb{Z}[A]\to\Pic X_{K^{\sep}}:a\to[D_{a}]
is injective. Its cokernel is
\Pic U_{K^{\sep}}
. Dually we get an exact sequence of groups of multiplicative type
1\to T_{\NS U}\to T_{\NS X}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}\to 1
.

The map
f:Y\to\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
is
T_{\NS X}
-equivariant, where
T_{\NS X}
acts on
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
through the inverse of the map
T_{\NS X}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
. Indeed this can be checked after base change to
K^{\sep}
in which case it can be reduced to the case when
D
is irreducible. The statement is an easy computation.

It follows from the construction that
\pi^{-1}(U)\cong f^{-1}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A})
. Each fiber of the map
f:\pi^{-1}(U)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
has a
T_{\NS U}
-action. Note also that if
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(A)(F)
and
g\in\text{im}(T_{\NS X}(K)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K))
then
g
there is an isomorphism between
f^{-1}(t)
and
f^{-1}(g\cdot t)
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K)
. Then
f^{-1}(t)\to U
is a universal torsor.

Moreover, if we fix for each
a\in\HH^{1}(K,T_{\NS})
a map
f_{a}:Y_{a}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
then every universal torsor of
U
is of the form
f^{-1}(t)
for some
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K)
. Moreover, the
t
is unique up to multiplication by an element of
\emph{im}(T_{\NS X}(K)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K))
.

Proof.

The quotient
\pi^{-1}(U)/T_{\NS U}\to U
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
-torsor because
\pi^{-1}(U)\to U
is a
T_{\NS}
-torsor. The map
\pi^{-1}(U)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
factors trough
\pi^{-1}(U)/T_{\NS U}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
and is
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
-equivariant. This implies that the torsor
\pi^{-1}(U)/T_{\NS U}\to U
is trivial so that the product map
\pi^{-1}(U)/T_{\NS U}\to U\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
is an isomorphism. Taking fibers of
t
we deduce that
f^{-1}(t)/T_{\NS U}\to U
is an isomorphism, i.e.
f^{-1}(t)\to U
is a
T_{\NS U}
-torsor.

Note that the above implies that
(f^{-1}(t)\times T_{\NS X})/T_{\NS U}\cong\pi^{-1}(U)
. We deduce that
\pi^{U}\to U
is the image of
f^{-1}(t)\to U
along the map
\HH^{1}(U,T_{\NS U})\to\HH^{1}(U,T_{\NS X})
.

The
T_{\NS X}
-torsor
\pi^{-1}(U)\to U
has type
\Pic X_{K^{\sep}}\to\Pic U_{K^{\sep}}
by functoriality of the type. We deduce from the above paragraph and the definition of the type that
f^{-1}(t)\to U
is a universal torsor.

To show the last statement it suffices to show that the twists of
f^{-1}(1)
are of always of the form
f_{a}^{-1}(t)
. Note that diagram
X\leftarrow Y\to\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
where
Y\to X
is a
T_{\NS X}
and
Y\to\mathbb{A}^{A}
is
T_{\NS X}
-equivariant has automorphism group
\ker(T_{\NS X}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A})=T_{\NS U}
.

If
b\in\HH^{1}(K,T_{\NS U})
then this implies that the twist of
f^{-1}(1)\to U
by
b
is the fiber of the rightmost map of the twist by
b
of the entire diagram
X\leftarrow Y\to\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
. The twist of underlying variety
Y
is
Y_{a}
where
a
is the image of
b
along
\HH^{1}(K,T_{\NS U}U)\to\HH^{1}(K,T_{\NS X})
. The group
T_{\NS U}
acts trivially on
\mathbb{A}^{A}
so the twist of this diagram is
X\leftarrow Y_{a}\xrightarrow{f_{b}}\mathbb{A}^{A}
.

The map
f_{b}
is not necessarily equal to
f_{a}
, the two maps can differ by an element of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K)
. The two diagrams
X\leftarrow Y_{a}\xrightarrow{f_{b}}\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
and
X\leftarrow Y_{a}\xrightarrow{f_{b}}\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
are isomorphic if and only they differ by an element of
T_{\NS X}(K)
.

We deduce that the twists of
X\leftarrow Y\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}
are all of the form
X\leftarrow Y_{a}\xrightarrow{t\cdot f_{a}}\mathbb{A}_{m}^{A}
with
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K)
unique up to multiplication by
\text{im}(T_{\NS X}(K)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{A}(K))
. The corresponding twist of
f^{-1}(1)
is given by
(t\cdot f_{a})^{-1}(1)=f_{a}^{-1}(t^{-1})
.
∎

Universal torsors can be used to study the Brauer-Manin obstruction, as was first observed by Colliot-Thélène and Sansuc
[
CTS87
]
for proper varieties
X
. This relation extends to adelic varieties

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\mathbf{X}
be an adelic variety over a number field
F
such that
\overline{F}_{v}[X_{v}]^{\times}=\overline{F}_{v}
and
\Pic(X_{v})_{\overline{F}_{v}}
is finitely generated for all places
v\in\Omega_{F}
. Let
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to\mathbf{X}
be a universal torsor. We then have

\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}}=\bigcup_{a\in H^{1}(F,T_{\NS,F})}\bm{\pi}_{a}(\mathbf{Y}_{a}(\mathbb{A}_{F})).

We will prove this completely analogously to the case of varieties
[
Sko01
, §6.1]
.

For
x_{v}\in X(F_{v})
let
Y_{v}(x_{v}):=\pi_{v}^{-1}(x_{v})
. This is a
T_{v}
-torsor over
F_{v}
, namely the unique
T_{v}
-torsor such that
x_{v}\in\pi_{Y_{v}(x_{v})}(F_{v})
, see
[
Sko01
, p. 22]
. If
T
has good reduction at
v
and
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
is an integral model of the
T_{v}
and
x_{v}\in\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
then
Y_{v}(x_{v})\in\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T_{v})
so taking restricted products of the
Y_{v}
induces a continuous map

(17)

\mathbf{Y}(\cdot):\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}).

Theorem
5.10
will follow from the following lemma.

Lemma 5.11
.

For
x\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
and
A\in\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}
we have
\langle x,A\rangle_{\emph{BM}}=\langle\mathbf{Y}(x),r(A)\rangle_{\emph{PT}}
.

Proof.

The bilinear pairings
T_{F}\times\Pic(X_{F})_{\overline{F}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
T_{v}\times\Pic(X_{v})_{\overline{F}_{v}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
for
v\in\Omega_{F}
induces compatible cup product pairings

\displaystyle\cdot\cup\;\cdot:\HH^{1}(F,\Pic(X_{F})_{\overline{F}})\times\HH^{1}(X,T_{F})\to\HH^{2}(X_{F},\mathbb{G}_{m})=\Br(X_{F})

\displaystyle\cdot\cup\;\cdot:\HH^{1}(F,\Pic(X_{v})_{\overline{F}_{v}})\times\HH^{1}(X,T_{v})\to\HH^{2}(X_{v},\mathbb{G}_{m})=\Br(X_{v}).

These pairings factor through
\Br_{1}X_{F}
and
\Br_{1}X_{v}
respectively since every cocycle in
\HH^{1}(F,\Pic(X_{F})_{\overline{F}})
and
\HH^{1}(F,\Pic(X_{v})_{\overline{F}_{v}})
becomes trivial after base change to the algebraic closure.

Consider the product pairing

\cdot\cup\;\cdot:\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X})\times\HH^{1}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{T})\to\Br_{1}(\mathbf{X}).

Note that
r(r(A)\cup[\mathbf{Y}])=r(A)
. Indeed, this means that
r([Y_{F}]\cup r(A_{F}))=r(A_{F})
and
r(r(A_{v})\cup[Y_{v}])=r(A_{v})
and these follow from
[
Sko01
, Thm. 4.1.1]
.

By Lemma
3.25
we deduce that
A=r(A)\cup[\mathbf{Y}]
in
\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}/\Br F
. We thus have

\langle x,A\rangle_{\text{BM}}=\langle x,r(A)\cup[\mathbf{Y}]\rangle_{\text{BM}}=\sum_{v\in\Omega_{F}}\text{inv}_{v}(r(A)\cup Y_{v}(x_{v}))=\langle r(A),\mathbf{Y}(x)\rangle_{\text{PT}}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.10
.

Let
x\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
. By Lemma
5.11
we have
x\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}}
if and only if
\langle\mathbf{Y}(x),r(A)\rangle_{\text{PT}}=0
for all
A\in\Br_{1}\mathbf{X}
. But
r
is surjective by Lemma
3.25
so this is equivalent to the equality
\langle\mathbf{Y}(x),b\rangle_{\text{PT}}=0
for all
b\in\HH^{1}(F,\bm{\Pic}\mathbf{X})
. We deduce from Proposition
3.29
that this is equivalent to the existence of an
a\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS,F})
such that
\mathbf{Y}(x)=a
, i.e.
x\in\pi_{a}(\mathbf{Y}_{a}(\mathbb{A}_{F}))
.
∎

5.3.
Tamagawa measures on universal torsors

In this subsection we will construct Tamagawa measures on universal torsors and show that they are compatible with Tamagawa measures on the base. We start with the local case.

5.3.1.
Local Tamagawa measures on universal torsors

Let
F
be a local field,
X
be a geometrically integral smooth variety over
F
. Assume that
\Pic X_{F^{\sep}}
is finitely generated and
F^{\sep}[X]^{\times}=F^{\sep,\times}
. Let
T_{\NS}
be the Néron-Severi torus and
\pi:Y\to X
be a universal torsor.

Let
D\subset X
be a geometrically simple normal crossing divisor and let
D_{1},\cdots,D_{n}
be the irreducible components of
D_{F^{\sep}}
. Let
Z:=\cap_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}
. This is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant and thus defined over
F
.

Let us first recall the structure of
\omega_{Y}
.

Lemma 5.12
.

There is a canonical isomorphism
\pi^{*}(\omega_{X}(D))\otimes\HH^{0}(T,\omega_{T})^{T}\cong\omega_{Y}(\pi^{-1}(D))
which is locally on
X
given by taking wedge products of differential forms.

We also have
\omega_{Y}(\pi^{-1}(D))\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
and
\HH^{0}(Y,\omega_{Y}(\pi^{-1}(D))^{\neq 0})\cong F^{\times}
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the fact that
\omega_{Y/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes\HH^{0}(T,\omega_{T})^{T}
by taking determinants in
[
Sal98
, Prop. 3.8, 3.9]
.

The second follows from the first and the fact that
F^{\sep}[Y]^{\times}\cong F^{\sep,\times}
and that the map
\Pic X\to\Pic Y
is zero, see
[
CTS87
, Prop. 2.1.1]
.
∎

Construction 5.13
.

Let
||\cdot||
be an adelic metric on
\omega_{X}
.

By repeated applications of the adjunction formula there is a canonical isomorphism
\omega_{\pi^{-1}(Z)}\cong\omega_{Y}(D)
, cf. §
4.3.1
.

Let
\omega\in\omega_{X}(D)^{\neq 0}(F)
. Then
\pi^{*}\omega\wedge d\log_{T}\in\omega_{Y}(\pi^{-1}(D))^{\neq 0}
by Lemma
5.12
. We can then use the isomorphism
\omega_{\pi^{-1}(Z)}\cong\omega_{Y}(D)
to define a local Tamagawa measure
\tau_{Y,||\cdot||}:=|\pi^{*}\omega\wedge d\log_{T}|/
on
\pi^{-1}(Z)(F)
. This is independent of
\omega
, cf. §
4.3.1
.

We will denote the pushforward of this measure along the closed inclusion
\pi^{-1}(Z)(F)\subset Y(F)
also by
\tau_{Y,\omega}
.

Since we can do the above construction for any universal torsor the above construction can be applied to define a measure
\cup_{a\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})}Y_{a}(F)
which we will denote by
\tau_{\cup_{a}Y_{a},\omega}
.

This measure is related to measures on the base.

Lemma 5.14
.

For every open
V\subset Z(F)
for which
\pi^{-1}(V)\cong V\times T_{\NS}(F)
one has the equality of measures

\tau_{Y,||\cdot||}(x)=(\tau_{X,||\cdot||}\times\tau_{T_{\NS}})(x).

Proof.

This follows immediately after unfolding the definitions.
∎

5.3.2.
Global Tamagawa measures on universal torsors

Let now
X
be a geometrically integral smooth proper variety over a number field
F
. Let
D\subset X
be a divisor with geometrically strict normal crossing and
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}
a face of the analytic Clemens complex. Let
U:=X\setminus D
and assume that
\overline{F}[U]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}

Assume that
\HH^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=\HH^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
. In particular
\Pic X
is torsion-free and finitely generated. Let
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}
be the Néron-Severi torus.

Let
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to(X,\mathbf{A})
be a universal torsor and let
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
be an adelic metric on this torsor with corresponding height
\mathbf{H}
. The line bundle
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\in\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
defines a character
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
. It follows from the definition of type that there exists a
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}
-equivariant map
\mathbf{Y}\to\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}
, unique up to multiplication by
F^{\times}
.

The adelic metric
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
thus defines an adelic metric on
\omega_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\neq 0}
, which by abuse of notation we will also denote by
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
. It is only well-defined up to multiplication by
F^{\times}
, but this does not influence the heights or the adelic Tamagawa measures.

We have the following bound on the local integrals.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
v
be a non-archimedean place such that
T_{\NS}
has good reduction and the
T_{\NS}
-torsor
Y\to U
has an
\mathcal{O}_{v}
-integral model
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{U}
such that
\mathcal{U}
is smooth and the metric
||\cdot||_{v}
is
\mathcal{U}
-integral. Let
a_{v}\in\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T_{\NS})
. Let
\pi_{v}:\mathcal{Y}_{a}\to\mathcal{U}
be a twist of
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{U}
. We have uniformly in
v
that

\tau_{Y_{v,a_{v}},||\cdot||_{v}}(\mathcal{Y}_{a_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v}))=1+O(q_{v}^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

Proof.

This follows from
[
CLT10a
, Thm 2.4.7, Rem. 2.4.12c)]
.
∎

Construction 5.16
.

Given
\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})
we define a measure on
\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
as the product

\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}=\prod_{v}\tau_{Y_{v,a_{v}},||\cdot||_{v}}.

This product converges by the previous lemma.

We also define a measure on
\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}(\#\pi_{0}((T_{\NS,v}))^{-1}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
as the product measure

\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}=\prod_{v}(\#\pi_{0}(T)(\mathcal{O}_{v}))^{-1}\tau_{\coprod_{a_{v}}Y_{v,a_{v}},||\cdot||_{v}}.

This measure converges by the previous Lemma and Lemma
3.31
.

Remark 5.17
.

We again use
\mathbf{H}
in the notation since these measures only depend on the height function
\mathbf{H}
by the same reasoning as in Lemma
4.23
.

Actually the same argument as in Lemma
4.23
shows in this case that
\mathbf{H}^{-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}
and
\mathbf{H}^{-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{\mathbf{}}{H}}}\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}
are independent of
\mathbf{H}
.

We have the following relation between these measures.

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
f:\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}
be a continuous
L^{1}
function with respect to the measure
\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}
. Then

\int_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(y)d\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}(y)=\int_{\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(y)d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}(y)d\mu_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(\mathbf{a})

Proof.

Both sides are infinite product measures over the same factors, because the convergence factor
\#\pi_{0}((T_{\NS,v})
appears both in
\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}
and
\mu_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(\mathbf{a})
. Both sides are thus integrals over the same measure and thus equal.
∎

We can also relate the measure
\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}
to the Tamagawa measure on the base
(X;\mathbf{A})
.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
V\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
be an open such that
\bm{\pi}^{-1}(V)\cong\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times V
. Over
V
we have the equality of measures

c_{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}=(\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{H}}\times\tau_{\mathbf{N}_{\NS}}).

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
5.14
and noticing that the convergence factors in
\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{H}}
and
\tau_{\mathbf{N}_{\NS}}
cancel with each other, except for the factor
c_{\mathbf{A}}
.
∎

An important consequence of this lemma is the following proposition.

Proposition 5.20
.

Let
f:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}
be an
L^{1}
continuous function with respect to the measure
\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{H}}
and
g:\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})\to\mathbb{C}
a continuous
L^{1}
function with respect to the measure
d\mathbf{z}_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}
. Then
f(\bm{\pi}(y))g(\log\mathbf{H}(y))
is
L^{1}
for
y\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)
with respect to the measure
\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}
and

\begin{split}&\int_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}f(\bm{\pi}(y))g(\log\mathbf{H}(y))d\tau_{\coprod_{\mathbf{a}}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{H}}(y)=\\
&\tau(\mathbf{T}_{\NS})\int_{(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(x)d\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{H}}(x)\int_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}g(z)d\mathbf{z}_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}.\end{split}

Proof.

By a partition of unity argument we may reduce to the case that
f
is supported on a
V\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
such that
\bm{\pi}^{-1}(V)\cong\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times V
.

By the previous lemma and Fubini we may then first integrate over the fibers of
\bm{\pi}
. Because
\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}
is a Haar measure the integral over the fiber is always equal to
\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}h(|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}
.

The integrand is
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
invariant so by Proposition
4.30
we have

\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}g(|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}=c_{\mathbf{A}}\tau(\mathbf{T}_{\NS})\int_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}g(z)d\mathbf{z}_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}.

The remaining integral over
V
is then exactly
\int_{(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(x)d\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{H}}(x)
as expected.
∎

5.3.3.
Gauge forms

Peyre showed that in the case of proper varieties the Tamagawa measure on the universal torsor can be interpreted in terms of Gauge forms of the universal torsor. In this part we will show how to extend this relation to the non-proper case. We keep the notation of the previous part.

6.
Integral Manin conjecture

Let
F
be a number field,
X
a geometrically integral smooth proper variety over
F
,
D\subset X
a geometrically strict normal crossing divisor and
U:=X\setminus D
. Let
H:U(F)\to\mathbb{R}
be a height function.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}(D)=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{v}(D)
be the
\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
-analytic Clemens complex of the pair
(X,D)
. Note that for every place
v
we have an open cover

X(F_{v})=\bigcup_{(A_{v},Z_{v})\in\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{v}}(X;A_{v},Z_{v})(F_{v})=\bigcup_{(A_{v},Z_{v})\in\mathcal{C}^{\an,\max}_{v}}(X;A_{v},Z_{v})(F_{v}).

Choose a compactly supported partition of unity with respect to this cover, i.e. choose for each
(A_{v},Z_{v})\in\mathcal{C}^{\an,\max}_{v}(D)
a compactly supported function
f_{(A_{v},Z_{v})}:(X;A_{v},Z_{v})(F_{v})\to[0,1]
such that
\sum_{(A_{v},Z_{v})\in\mathcal{C}^{\an,\max}_{v}}f_{(A_{v},Z_{v})}(x_{v})=1
for all
x_{v}\in X(F_{v})
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be an integral model of
U
and for each non-archimedean place
v
let
f_{v}:U(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}
be the indicator function of the set
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
.

For a maximal face
\mathbf{A}=((A_{v},Z_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}^{\infty})\in\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{\Omega_{F}}
we define the product

f_{\mathbf{A}}:=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}f_{(A_{v},Z_{v})}:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to[0,1].

This is a compactly supported continuous function.

By construction we have the following equality for all
T>0

(18)

\#\{P\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{O}_{F}):H(P)\leq T\}=\sum_{\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{\an,\max}_{\Omega_{F}}}\sum_{P\in U(F),H(P)\leq T}f_{\mathbf{A}}(x).

To study integral points of bounded height it will thus suffice to study the sums
\sum_{P\in U(F),H(P)\leq T}f_{\mathbf{A}}(x).
We will make a conjecture on the asymptotic behavior of such sums.

If there is an analytic obstruction near
\mathbf{A}
then the integral points counted by weighting by
f_{\mathbf{A}}
are not Zariski dense by Theorem
3.13
. We will thus ignore such faces.

6.1.
The conjecture

Let
X,D,U
be as above and let
\mathbf{A}\in\mathcal{C}^{\an}_{\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}(D)
be a face which has no analytic obstruction, i.e.
\HH^{0}((X;\mathbf{A}),\mathcal{O}_{(X;\mathbf{A})})=F
.

Assume that
\Pic X
is finitely generated. Then
\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
is finitely generated by Lemma
3.10
. The pseudo-effective cone
\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\subset(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies
\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\cap-\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}=0
by Proposition
3.16
.

Let
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\in\Pic(X,\mathbf{A})
be the image of the log canonical divisor
K_{(X,D)}:=K_{X}+D
along the map
\Pic X\to\Pic(X,\mathbf{A})
. Assume that
-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
is
big
, i.e.
-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
. This is true for example if
-K_{(X,D)}\in\Pic X
is big. Let
L\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
be a big line bundle. Define the
Fujita invariant
as

a:=a((X;\mathbf{A}),L):=\inf\{t\in\mathbb{R}:K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}+tL\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\}.

Assume moreover that the pseudo-effective cone
\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
is a polyhedral cone. This is true if
\Eff_{X}
is polyhedral, which holds if
(X,D)
is log Fano by
[
BCHM10
, Cor. 1.3.5]
. Then define
b:=b((X;\mathbf{A}),L)
as the codimension of the minimal face of the cone
\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
which contains the
adjoint divisor

K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}+a((X;\mathbf{A}),L)L
.

Conjecture 6.1
.

Assume that
(X,D)
is log Fano and
\overline{F}[U]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}
. Let
L\in\Eff_{(\mathbf{X};\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}\cap\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
be a big and nef line bundle,
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
an adelic metric on the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor corresponding to
L
and
H:U(F)\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}
the induced height function.

There exists a thin subset
Z\subset U(F)
such that the following holds: if
f:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is a compactly supported continuous function which is non-zero when restricted to the closure
\overline{U(F)\setminus Z}\cap U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
then there exists a positive constant
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
such that as
T\to\infty
we have

(19)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in U(F)\setminus Z\\
H(P)\leq T\end{subarray}}f(P)\sim c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)T^{a((X;\mathbf{A}),L)}(\log T)^{b((X;\mathbf{A}),L)-1}.

Remark 6.2
.

(1)

The assumption that the pair
(X,D)
is log Fano can be weakened, this is already true for the usual Manin conjecture. Since it is unclear what the precise geometric condition one needs for
X
in the usual Manin conjecture we will just make the log Fano assumption.

(2)

There are examples of varieties
U
for which the integral Manin conjecture holds and whose Picard group has torsion, e.g. Example
6.3.3
. In this case
U
has a finite étale cover which implies that for every compact set
C\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
the intersection
U(F)\cap C
is thin. To get a non-trivial conjecture we thus have to assume that the thin set
Z
is independent of
f
.

(3)

The assumption that the restriction of
f
to the closure
\overline{U(F)\setminus Z}
is non-zero is necessary since the left-hand side of (
19
) is
0
otherwise.

(4)

If
L=-a((X;\mathbf{A}),L)K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
then
b((X;\mathbf{A}),L)=\text{rk}\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})=\text{rk}\Pic U+\dim\mathbf{A}+1
by Corollary
3.12
.

(5)

We expect that there should also be a conjecture without assuming that
\overline{F}[U]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}
. But in this case one should give a different definition of
\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
, see
[
Wil22
, Def. 2.2.1]
for a definition in the split case.

6.2.
Leading constant

In this section we will give a conjectural interpretation of leading constant in (
19
) assuming that the algebraic Brauer-Manin obstruction is the only obstruction. We keep the notations of Conjecture
6.1
.

Peyre’s constant is easiest to define in the case that
L
is
adjoint rigid
. In the case that
L
is not adjoint rigid one has to take into account the
Iitaka fibration
6
6

6

The terminology Iitaka fibration is now standard in Manin’s conjecture literature, but Batyrev and Tschinkel instead used the word
\mathcal{L}
-primitive fibration.
as in
[
BT98b
]
. It is unclear to us what the analogue of the Iitaka fibration is for integral points so we will assume that
L
satisfies the analogue of adjoint rigidity.

Definition 6.3
.

By Lemma
3.10
we can write
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}+a((X;\mathbf{A}),L)L
as a sum

\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}[E_{i}]+\sum_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\sum_{j_{v}=1}^{m}d_{j_{v}}[D_{j_{v},v}].

Where
E_{i}\subset X
are irreducible divisors not contained in
D
,
D_{j_{v},v}\subset D_{F_{v}}
are irreducible divisors containing
Z_{v}
and
c_{i},d_{j_{v}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. We say that
L
is
adjoint rigid
if this decomposition is unique.

Let
U_{L}:=U\setminus(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}E_{i})
and
U_{A_{v},L}:=(X;A_{v},Z_{v})\setminus\Big(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}E_{i}\cup(\bigcup_{j_{v}=1}^{m_{v}}D_{j_{v}})\Big)
for all places
v
. Consider the adelic variety
(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}:=(U_{L},(U_{A_{v},L})_{v})
. We will need the following properties.

Lemma 6.4
.

If
L
is adjoint rigid then we have

(1)

\overline{F}[U_{L}]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}
.

(2)

The map
\Pic U_{\overline{F}}\to\Pic U_{L,\overline{F}}
is an isomorphism on torsion subgroups.

(3)

\HH^{0}((X;\mathbf{A})_{L},\mathcal{O}_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}})=F
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}
denote the
\Gamma_{F}
-set of irreducible components of
(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}E_{i})_{\overline{F}}
. We have an exact sequence of
\Gamma_{F}
-modules

(20)

\overline{F}[U_{L}]^{\times}/\overline{F}[U]^{\times}\to\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{E}]\to\Pic U_{\overline{F}}\to\Pic U_{L,\overline{F}}\to 0.

The first two statements will thus follow as long as
\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{E}]\to(\Pic U_{\overline{F}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
is injective. If the map is not injective then there exists two non-trivial elements
e_{1},e_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}[\mathcal{E}]
which becoming equal in
\Pic U_{\overline{F}}
. Taking norms we get a non-trivial relation in
\Pic U
between the
[E_{i}]
which contradicts adjoint rigidity.

For the last statement let
\mathbf{g}\in\HH^{0}((X;\mathbf{A})_{L},\mathcal{O}_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}})
be non-constant. Since
\HH^{0}((X;\mathbf{A}),\mathcal{O}_{(X;\mathbf{A})})=F
the function
\mathbf{g}
must have a pole at one of the
E_{i}
or
D_{j_{v},v}
. But this implies that a non-trivial non-negative linear combination of the
[E_{i}]
and
[D_{j_{v},v}]
is equal to
\mathbf{g}^{-1}(0)
. This contradicts adjoint rigidity.
∎

Assume from now on that
L
is adjoint rigid.

Let
\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}
be the Néron-Severi torus of
(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}
. We have a map
\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}\to\mathbf{T}_{\NS}
coming from the inclusion
(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}\subset(X;\mathbf{A})
.

Lemma 6.5
.

The induced map
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS L})\to\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})
of topological groups is proper, i.e. inverse images of compact subsets are compact.

Proof.

For the places
v
of good reduction we will show that
\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T_{\NS L})\to\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T_{\NS})
is an isomorphism. This suffices for the lemma by the definition of the topology on
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS L})
and
\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})
.

Both groups have the same size by Lemma
3.31
and Lemma
6.4
:(2) The map is also surjective, as one sees by combining the exactness of the sequence
1\to T_{\NS L}\to T_{\NS}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathcal{E}}\to 1
dual to (
20
) and Lemma
2.7
.
∎

Let
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}_{L}\to(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}
be a universal torsor and

\mathbf{H}:\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS L})}\mathbf{Y}_{L,\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\cong(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})_{L})_{\mathbb{R}}^{*}

a height function extending
H
, it exists by Lemma
4.12
.

Note that
aL=-K_{(X,\mathbf{A})}=-K_{(X,\mathbf{A})_{L}}
in
\Pic(X,\mathbf{A})_{L}
by construction. So the Tamagawa measure
\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}
is well-defined.

Conjecture 6.6
.

Assume that
L
is adjoint rigid and that the only obstruction to strong approximation for
(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}
is the algebraic Brauer-Manin obstruction.

The leading constant
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
in Conjecture
6.1
is then equal to

\frac{1}{a(b-1)!}\sum_{\alpha\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS L})}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y))H^{-1}(y)f(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}(y).

Remark 6.7
.

(1)

This sum is finite by Lemma
6.5
.

(2)

The equality between this constant and Peyre’s constant for rational points and if
L=-K_{X}
is implicit in Salberger’s work
[
Sal98
, Assertion 5.25]
and follows from work of Peyre
[
Pey98
, Thm. 5.3.1]
. Note that the measure in Peyre’s work is different to Salberger’s measure, we use the latter. See
[
Pey98
, Rmk. 4.4.1]
for a comparison.

Let us note the following compatibility when changing height functions.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
g:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Hom(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})_{L},\mathbb{R}^{\times})
be a continuous function with
\mathbf{H}:=g\cdot\mathbf{H}^{\prime}
and
H^{\prime}:=\mathbf{H}^{\prime L}
. Then

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y))H^{-1}(y)f(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}(y)=\\
&\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}^{\prime}(y))H^{\prime-1}(y)f(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))g(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))^{-aL}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{\prime-a}}(y).\end{split}

Proof.

Now note that
\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\mathfrak{b}},H^{\prime-a}}
is
T_{\NS L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-invariant by Lemma
5.14
so we can make the change of variables
y^{\prime}=g(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))y
to see that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y^{\prime}))H^{-1}(y^{\prime})f(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y^{\prime}))d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}(y^{\prime})=\\
&\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(yg(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))))H^{-1}(yg(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y)))f(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}(y).\end{split}

The lemma then follows because
\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}(y)=g(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))^{-aL}\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{\prime-a}}(y)
by Lemma
4.23
and
\mathbf{H}(yg(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))=g(\bm{\pi}_{\alpha}(y))^{-1}\mathbf{H}(y)=\mathbf{H}^{\prime}(y)
.
∎

Remark 6.9
.

(1)

The extension
\mathbf{H}
only matters due to the presence of
\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y))
. It follows from the previous lemma that the constant is independent of this extension.

(2)

If we replace
H
by
CH
for
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
then this corresponds to replacing
\mathbf{H}
by
g\mathbf{H}
with
g
constant and
g(L)=C
. It follows that
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,CH)=C^{-a}c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
, so Conjecture
6.6
is compatible with multiplication of heights by constants.

6.2.1.
Brauer-Manin set

We can relate this leading constant to Peyre’s usual constant defined in terms of the Tamagawa measure of the Brauer-Manin set in good cases. Let use recall the definition of the effective cone constant

Definition 6.10
.

Let
L\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
be a big and adjoint rigid divisor. We define the
effective cone constant
as
\theta((X;\mathbf{A}),L):=\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}}(L)
.

Theorem 6.11
.

If the sum

\tau((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H):=\sum_{\beta\in\Br_{1}(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}/\Br F}\int_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}e^{2\pi i\langle x,\beta\rangle_{\emph{BM}}}f(x)d\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L},H^{-a}}(x).

converges absolutely then
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)=\frac{\theta((X;\mathbf{A}),L)}{a(b-1)!}\tau((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
.

Remark 6.12
.

In the case that the sum
\tau((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
