converges absolutely we can rewrite it as the following limit over finite subgroups
\Br_{1}(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}/\Br F

\lim_{B\subset\Br_{1}(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}/\Br F}\#B\int_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{B}}f(x)d\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L},H^{-a}}(x).

This is true since
\sum_{\beta\in B}e^{2\pi i\langle x,\beta\rangle_{\emph{BM}}}
is equal to
\#B
if
x\in(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{B}
and
0
otherwise by orthogonality of characters.

If
\Br_{1}(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}/\Br F
is finite then the sum is finite and equal to

\int_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\Br_{1}(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}}f(x)d\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L},H^{-a}}(x)

which agrees with the prediction of Batyrev and Tschinkel when
U=X

[
BT98b
]
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.25
and Lemma
5.11
we have

\tau((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H):=\sum_{\beta\in\HH^{1}(\Pic((X;\mathbf{A}),L))}\int_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}e^{2\pi i\langle\mathbf{Y}_{L}(x),\beta\rangle_{\emph{BM}}}f(x)d\tau_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L},H^{-a}}(x).

By Proposition
5.20
, Proposition
5.18
and the definition of
\theta((X;\mathbf{A}),L)
we can rewrite
\theta((X;\mathbf{A}),L)\tau((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
as

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\tau(\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}&\sum_{\beta\in\HH^{1}(\Pic((X;\mathbf{A}),L))}\int_{\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS L})}e^{2\pi i\langle\mathbf{Y}_{L}(x),\beta\rangle_{\emph{BM}}}\\
&\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(\pi_{\mathbf{a}}(y))\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y))\mathbf{H}(y)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\mathbf{a}},H^{-a}}(y)d\mu_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}}(\mathbf{a}).\end{split}

The inner integral is by definition the Fourier transform of the function

\HH^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS L})\to\mathbb{C}:\mathbf{a}\to\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\mathbf{a}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(\pi_{\mathbf{a}}(y))\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y))\mathbf{H}(y)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\mathbf{a}},H^{-a}}(y).

This function is compactly supported by Lemma
6.5
so the Poisson summation formula holds. By Corollary
3.37
we find that
\theta((X;\mathbf{A}),L)\tau((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
is equal to

\sum_{\alpha\in\HH^{1}(F,\mathbf{T}_{\NS L})}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}f(\pi_{a}(y))\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})_{L}}^{*}}(\log\mathbf{H}(y))\mathbf{H}(y)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y}_{L,\alpha},H^{-a}}(y).

This is exactly what we had to prove.
∎

6.3.
Examples

If
\Br_{1}(X;\mathbf{A})/\Br F\subset\Br_{1}U/\Br F
is trivial then Theorem
6.11
shows that Conjecture
6.1
agrees with the prediction in
[
Wil22
, §2.5]
. As explained in loc. cit. this shows that our prediction agrees with the previously known results in
[
CLT12
,
TBT13
,
Cho19
,
Wil22
,
DW22
,
Wil23
]
.

6.3.1.
Wilsch’s counterexample

Let us explain what happens in Wilsch’s counterexample
[
Wil22
]
to Chambert-Loir and Tschinkel’s work
[
CLT10b
]
. We will use the notation of Wilsch, so we have a toric variety
X
and a boundary divisor
D\subset X
with three irreducible components
M,E_{1},E_{2}
. The Clemens complex has
4
non-trivial elements
M,E_{1},E_{2}
and
A:=E_{1}\cap E_{2}
.

Wilsch shows in
[
Wil22
, §3.3]
that the pair
(X,D)
has an analytic obstruction near the face
A
and thus also near the faces
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
. The face
M
is thus the only non-trivial face with no analytic obstruction. We have
\Pic(X;M)\cong\mathbb{Z}^{3}
by
[
Wil22
, Prop. 3.3.1]
so the expected order of growth is
B(\log B)^{2}
which agrees with
[
Wil22
, Thm. 1.0.1]
.

We have that
(X;M)
is
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{3}
minus the two lines
\{a_{1}=b_{1}=0\}
and
\{a_{1}=c_{1}=0\}
which intersect in a point. Wilsch only counts integral points for which one always has
a_{1}/a_{0}\geq 1
so is implicitly weighting by a compactly supported function
(X;M)(\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
. He shows in Lemma 3.3.4 that the leading constant can be interpreted as a Tamagawa measure. By Theorem
6.11
this agrees with Conjecture
6.6
. Actually, the leading constant can be shown to agree with Conjecture
6.6
directly from
[
Wil22
, Lemma 3.2.1]
without computing Tamagawa measures on
U
. We leave this to the interested reader.

6.3.2.
Counterexample when there are global invertible sections

Let
X=\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
D
a union of two lines. The pair
(X,D)
is log Fano but
U\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. If
k=\mathbb{Q}
then the integral points
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}(\mathbb{Z})=\{-1,1\}\times\mathbb{Z}
are not even Zariski-dense. One checks that this is not due to an analytic obstruction.

The integral points will be Zariski-dense for number fields
F
with at least two archimedean places. In this case one can count integral points of bounded anticanonical height on
U
. But to do this one has to count integral points on the torus
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The number of integral points on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
of bounded height has order of magnitude a power of
\log T
by Dirichlet’s unit theorem. Such counting problems are not very similar to those appearing in Manin’s conjecture, this is why we make the assumption that
\overline{F}^{\times}[U]=\overline{F}^{\times}
in Conjecture
6.1
.

6.3.3.
Infinite Brauer group

Let
U:=\Spec\mathbb{Q}[x,y,z]/(xy=z^{2})\setminus\{x=y=z=0\}
. This is a smooth toric variety with no invertible global sections,
\Pic U\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
and
\Br U\cong\HH^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Q}^{\times}/\mathbb{Q}^{\times 2}
is infinite. One can count integral points on this variety. Indeed, if
\mathcal{U}:=\Spec\mathbb{Z}[x,y,z]/(xy=z^{2})\setminus\{x=y=z=0\}
is the obvious integral model then integral points on
\mathcal{U}
correspond to triples
(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
with
\gcd(x,y,z)=1
. This implies that integral points are of the form
(\pm u^{2},\pm v^{2},uv)
for coprime
u,v\in\mathbb{Z}
7
7

7

The map
\mathbb{A}^{2}_{u,v}\to U:(u,v)\to(u^{2},uv,v^{2})
is a universal torsor of
U
.
, well-defined up to multiplication by
-1
. The log-anticanonical height is
\max(u^{2},v^{2})
.

We see that both
x
and
y
have to be
\pm
a square in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all primes
p
. This gives an infinite number of local conditions so
\overline{\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z})}\subset\prod_{v}\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
will not be open. If
\tau
is the usual Tamagawa measure on
\prod_{v}\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
then
\tau(\overline{\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z})})=0
.

6.3.4.
Effect of different faces

In the previous examples there was a only a single relevant face. In the following example one can see the effect of counting near different faces.

Let
X
be the projective plane
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x,y,z}
blown up at the point
\{x=z=0\}
and the point
\{y=z=0\}
. Let
E_{x},E_{y}\subset X
be the respective exceptional divisors and
D
the proper transform of
\{z=0\}
. Consider the pair
(X,E_{x}\cup E_{y}\cup D)
, it is not log Fano but the anticanonical divisor is big and an analogue of Manin’s conjecture still holds. In this case
U=\mathbb{A}^{2}_{x,y}
so for the standard integral model
\mathcal{U}=\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}}
integral points correspond to pairs
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The log anticanonical height is given by
\max(|xy|,1)
. The Clemens complex has five non-trivial faces, let us discuss the integral points of bounded height close to each of them.

(1)

The maximal face
Z=E_{x}\cup D
has dimension
1
. In this case
U_{Z}=X\setminus E_{y}
. Weighting by a compactly supported function
f:U_{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
thus causes one to only count those
x,y
for which
x=O(y)
. The order of magnitude in this case is
T\log T
. The other maximal face
E_{y}\cup D
is analogous.

(2)

Another face is
D
which has dimension
0
. In this case
U_{D}=X\setminus(E_{x}\cup E_{y})=\mathbb{P}^{2}\setminus(\{x=z=0\}\cup\{y=z=0\})
. Weighting by a compactly supported function
f:U_{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
thus cause one to only count pairs
x,y
such that
x=O(y)
and
y=O(x)
. The order of magnitude is
T
.

(3)

For the face
E_{x}
we have
U_{E_{x}}=X\setminus(D\cup E_{y})\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Weighting by a compactly supported function
f:U_{Z}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
corresponds to only counting those
(x,y)
with
y=O(1)
. The collection of such points is not Zariski dense. This is due to an analytic obstruction.

Note that counting all integral points by only counting those close to faces corresponds to separating the count into two cases, in the first one
x=O(y)
and in the second one
y=O(x)
. This is known as the Dirichlet hyperbola method and it is the standard method to count pairs
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|xy|\leq T
.

6.3.5.
The circle method

The circle method is a very powerful tool to count integral and rational points on varieties of large relative dimension to the degree, as worked out in Birch’s seminal work
[
Bir62
]
. It is well-known
[
Pey95
, Prop. 4.2.1]
that the circle method is compatible with Manin’s conjecture for rational points, including Peyre’s prediction for the leading constant. Similar arguments show that the circle method is also compatible with Conjectures
6.1
and
6.6
. Let us work this out in detail in the work of Skinner
[
Ski97
]
on integral points on affine complete intersections.

Let
g_{1},\cdots,g_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}[X_{1},\cdots,X_{s}]
be a collection of polynomials of degree
d
with
s>m+3
. Let
G_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[X_{0},\cdots,X_{s}]
the homogenization of
g_{i}
and let
h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[X_{1},\cdots,X_{s}]
be the degree
d
part of
g_{i}
. We want to understand integral points of bounded height on
U:=\{g_{1}=\cdots=g_{l}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{s}
. We will consider the compactification
U\subset X:=\{G_{1}=\cdots=G_{m}=0\}\subset\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{s}
. Let
D:=X\setminus U=\{h_{1}=\cdots=h_{m}=0\}\subset\{X_{0}=0\}=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{s-1}
. We will assume that
X
and
D
are smooth complete intersections,
D
is in particular geometrically irreducible.

The analytic Clemens complex has a single non-trivial face
D
. Let
\mathcal{U}
be the obvious integral model of
U
. Let
f
to be the indicator function of
\prod_{p}\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times X(\mathbb{R})
.

One has
\Pic X=\mathbb{Z}
generated by
[D]
by the Lefschetz hyperplane theorem so
\Pic(X;D)=\mathbb{Z}
. This implies that
\Br_{1}(X;D)=\Br_{1}U=\Br\mathbb{Q}
. The canonical divisor is
K_{X}=(md-s-1)[D]
and the log anti-canonical divisor is
-K_{(X,D)}=(s-md)[D]
, which is ample. The adelic universal torsor
\mathbf{Y}
of
(X;D)
is given by
U
at the primes and by
\{g_{1}=\cdots=g_{n}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{s+1}_{\mathbb{Q}}\setminus\{0\}
at
\infty
. Let
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathbb{R}^{s}
be a compact neighbourhoud of the origin and define an adelic metric on the universal torsor by setting
||\cdot||_{p}=1
for all primes and
||(x_{0},\cdots,x_{s})||_{\infty}=\inf(t\in\mathbb{R}:(x_{1},\cdots,x_{s})\in t\mathcal{B})^{-1}
for
(x_{0},\cdots,x_{s})\in Y_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\subset\mathbb{R}^{s+1}
. Let
H=||(x_{0},\cdots,x_{s})||_{\infty}^{-1}
be the corresponding height.

Skinner
[
Ski97
, Main Thm.]
shows
8
8

8

Skinner actually only works with
\mathcal{B}
which are box shaped, the case of arbitrary
\mathcal{B}
follows from
[
Lou15
, Lemma 4.9]
.
via the circle method that if
s\geq m(m+1)(d-1)2^{d-1}
then there exists a
c>0
such that

(21)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in U(\mathbb{Q})\\
H(P)\leq T\end{subarray}}f(P)=\#\{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{s})\in\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z})\cap T\mathcal{B}\}\sim cT^{s-md}.

This agrees with Conjecture
6.1
.

The adelic universal torsor of
(X;D)
is given by
U
at the non-archimedean places and by
\{G_{1}=\cdots=G_{n}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{s+1}_{\mathbb{Q}}\setminus\{0\}
at the archimedean places. Let
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathbb{R}^{s}
be a compact s Let
du\in\omega_{U}
be the differential form on
U
which is the image of the differential form
g_{1}^{-1}\cdots g_{m}^{-1}dX_{1}\cdots dX_{s}
on
\mathbb{A}^{s}_{\mathbb{Q}}
under the adjunction formula for the immersion
U\subset\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{s}
. Similarly let
dy
be the differential form on
Y\cap\{X_{0}=0\}
which is the image of
G_{1}^{-1}\cdots G_{m}^{-1}X_{0}^{-1}dX_{0}dX_{1}\cdots dX_{s}
under the adjunction formula for the immersion
Y\cap\{X_{0}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{s+1}
. These differential forms are clearly compatible.

The equality of
\int_{\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}|du|_{p}=\sigma_{p}
where
\sigma_{p}:=\lim_{k\to\infty}p^{-k\dim U}\#\{\mathbf{x}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})^{s}:g_{i}(\mathbf{x})=0\}
is the local density in the singular series is
[
BR95
, Lemma 1.8.1]
. The singular integral
[
Ski97
, p. 23]
is more interesting. The complete intersection is non-singular so this integral is by
[
Bir62
, Lemma 6.3]
equal to

\sigma_{\infty}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}e^{2i\pi\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{x})}d\mathbf{x}dt_{1}\cdots dt_{m}=\int_{Y(\mathbb{R})_{D}=(Y\cap\{X_{0}=0\})(\mathbb{R})}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}}(y)|dy|_{\infty}.

We see that the leading constant predicted in Conjecture
6.6
is
\frac{1}{s-md}\prod_{p}\sigma_{p}
times
\int_{Y(\mathbb{R})_{D}}\mathbf{1}_{\{H(y)>1\}}(y)H(y)^{-s+md-1}|dy|_{\infty}
. To compare this with the singular integral we note that for
t\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
we have
|dty|_{\infty}=t^{s-md}|dy|_{\infty}
so
\text{Vol}(Y(\mathbb{R})_{D}\cap t\mathcal{B}):=\int_{Y(\mathbb{R})_{D}}\mathbf{1}_{t\mathcal{B}}(y)|dy|_{\infty}=t^{s-md}\sigma_{\infty}
. We then have

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\mathbb{R})_{D}}\mathbf{1}_{\{H(y)>1\}}(y)H(y)^{-s+md-1}|dy|_{\infty}=\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{-s+md-1}\frac{d}{dt}\text{Vol}(Y(\mathbb{R})_{D}\cap t\mathcal{B})dt=

\displaystyle(s-md)\sigma_{\infty}\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{-2}dt=(s-md)\sigma_{\infty}.

We conclude that the leading constant
c:=c_{\infty}\prod_{p}c_{p}
in (
21
) agrees with Conjecture
6.6
.

Remark 6.13
.

Skinner in
[
Ski97
]
proves a counting result which is valid over all number fields and not only over
\mathbb{Q}
. But the height he uses is not a height in our sense so we were unfortunately unable to interpret this result in the framework of this paper.

6.4.
Reductions

It is often convenient to first prove Manin’s conjecture for simpler
f
and particular heights
H
and then deduce the general conjecture. Since these reductions are rather formal we collect them here.

Proposition 6.14
.

Assume that Conjecture
6.1
holds with the constant conjectured in Conjecture
6.6
for one particular height
H
and for all
f=\prod_{v}f_{v}
where for each
v\in\Omega_{F}
the function
f_{v}:(X;A_{v},Z_{v})(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is compactly supported and is locally constant, resp. smooth, if
v
is non-archimedean, resp. archimedean.

Conjecture
6.1
with the constant conjectured in Conjecture
6.6
then holds for all possible heights on
L
and all compactly supported functions
f
.

Proof.

If Conjecture
6.6
holds for
f
of the form
\prod_{v}f_{v}
then it also holds for linear combinations of such functions. The vector space of such linear combinations is dense in the vector space of compactly supported continuous functions.

Let
f:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be any compactly supported continuous function. We may then uniformly approximate
f
both from below and from above by functions for which Conjecture
6.6
holds. Taking limits of both these approximations gives the desired statement.

That Conjecture
6.6
then holds for all possible heights is essentially due to Peyre
[
Pey95
, Prop. 3.3(b)]
. Let us repeat the argument in our context for completeness. Any other height
H^{\prime}
is equal to
gH
for
g:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
a continuous function. For every
\varepsilon>0
we can write
f
as a sum of continuous compactly supported functions
f_{i}:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that there exists a
c_{i}>0
and such that
c_{i}-\varepsilon<g(x)<c_{i}+\varepsilon
for all
x
contained in the support of
f_{i}
by a partition of unity argument.

The following inequality follows from Conjecture
6.1
as
T\to\infty

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in U(F)\setminus Z\\
(c_{i}-\varepsilon)T\leq H(P)\leq(c_{i}+\varepsilon)T\end{subarray}}f_{i}(P)\ll\varepsilon(c_{i}T)^{a-1}(\log c_{i}T)^{b}.

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
we are reduced to the case when
g
is constant. This case follows from Lemma
6.8
, cf. Remark
6.9
.
∎

7.
Counting

7.1.
Set-up

Let
F
be a number field,
X
a smooth proper toric variety over
F
with corresponding torus
T
and
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant fan
\Sigma
on the lattice
N:=\Hom(\mathbb{G}_{m},T)
. Let
Z\subset X
be a toric divisor corresponding to a set of rays
Z_{\min}\subset\Sigma_{\min}
. Let
U:=X\setminus Z
. We will assume that
\overline{F}[U]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}
. The complement
D:=\Sigma_{\min}\setminus Z_{\min}
is the
\Gamma_{F}
-set of irreducible toric divisors of
U
.

Let
\mathbf{A}=(A_{v},Z_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\text{an}}_{\Omega^{\infty}}(D)
be a face of the analytic Clemens complex. Note that
A_{v}\subset D_{\min}
is the set of divisors containing
Z_{v}
for all
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
. We will write
U_{A_{v}}:=(X;A_{v},Z_{v})
. We assume that
\HH^{0}((X;\mathbf{A}),\mathcal{O}_{(X;\mathbf{A})})
. This implies by Proposition
3.16
that the pseudo-effective cone
\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\subset(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{R}}
does not contain a line.

Assume furthermore that
-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
. Note that by (
3
) we have

(22)

-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}=\sum_{D_{\rho}\in D}[D_{\rho}].

7.1.1.
Universal torsor

Let
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}=(T_{\NS},(T_{\NS A_{v}})_{v})
be the Néron-Severi torus of
(X;\mathbf{A})
.

Let
\pi:Y\to U
, resp.
\pi_{v}:Y_{A_{v}}\to U_{A_{v}}
for each place
v
, be the universal constructed in Construction
2.9
.

There is a natural toric inclusions
Y_{F_{v}}\subset Y_{A_{v}}
so
\mathbf{Y}:=(Y,(Y_{A_{v}})_{v})
is an adelic variety and
\bm{\pi}:\mathbf{Y}\to(X,\mathbf{A})
is a universal torsor.

Let
\Theta=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D}
and
\Theta_{A_{v}}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D\cup A_{v}}
. Let
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}:=(\Theta,(\Theta_{A_{v}})_{v})
be an adelic torus. It is by construction equal to
\bm{\pi}^{-1}(T)\subset\mathbf{Y}
.

Fix a smooth adelic metric
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
on the torsor
\bm{\pi}
. By enlarging
S
we may assume that
(||\cdot||_{v})_{v\in\Omega_{F}}
has good reduction at all places
v\not\in S
.
Note that
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}
acts via the inverse of the inclusion
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}
. So if
t\in\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
and
x\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
then
||t\cdot x||=t^{-1}||x||\in\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{O}})
.

Note that
\hat{\Theta}_{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbb{Z}[D/\Gamma_{F}]\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{Z}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]
. Given
c,d\in\mathbb{C}
we denote by
(\mathbf{c},\mathbf{d})\in\hat{\Theta}_{\mathbf{A},\mathbb{R}}
the element
\sum_{\rho\in D/\Gamma_{F}}c\cdot[\rho]+\sum_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty},\rho_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}}d\cdot[\rho_{v}]
and
\mathbf{c}=(\mathbf{c},\mathbf{c})
. We will use the same notation for the images of these elements in
\hat{\mathbf{T}}_{\NS}
.

We will also use the analogous notation for elements
\hat{\Theta}_{A_{v}}=\mathbb{Z}[(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}]
for all places
v
.

Let
\mathbf{H}:\coprod_{\mathbf{a}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{F},\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Hom(\Pic(X,\mathbf{A}),\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times})
be the corresponding height function. If
t\in\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
and
x\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
then
\mathbf{H}(t\cdot x)=|t|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}\mathbf{H}(x)
.

The toric divisors generate the effective cone so by Corollary
4.20
we may assume without loss of generality that that
\log\mathbf{H}(x)\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{*}
for all
x\in T(F)
by multiplying the metric and thus the height by a constant.

7.1.2.
The function

Let
S
be a finite set of places containing all the archimedean places, all ramified places of
K/\mathbb{Q}
and all the places of bad reduction of
T,\Theta
and
T_{\NS}
. Let
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{U}
an
\mathcal{O}_{K,S}
-integral model of the
T_{\NS}
torsor
Y\to U
such that
\mathcal{U}
, resp.
\mathcal{Y}
, is equipped with an integral model of the action of
T
, resp.
\Theta
. This exists by spreading out, after potentially enlarging
S
.

For each place
v
of
F
let
f_{v}:U_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be a compactly supported function. Assume that
f_{v}
is smooth if
v
is archimedean and locally constant if
v
is non-archimedean. We will assume that if
v\not\in S
then
f_{v}
is the indicator function of
\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
. The product
f:=\prod_{v}f_{v}:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is then a well-defined compactly supported function.

7.1.3.
Smoothing the height

To ensure that certain integrals converge absolutely it will be convenient to smooth the height function.

Construction 7.1
.

For
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\text{fin}}
let
\varphi_{v}:T_{\NS}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}
be the indicator function of
T_{\NS}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
and for each place
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
fix a non-zero smooth compactly supported function
\varphi_{v}:T_{\NS A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}
. The product function
\varphi:=\prod_{v}\varphi_{v}:\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}
is then compactly supported.

By shifting the
\varphi_{v}
we may assume that if
y\in\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
is such that
\varphi(y)\neq 0
then
\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{*}
.

We will consider the following smoothed form of the height function
\mathbf{H}
. For
\bm{s}\in(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{C}}
let
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(\cdot;\bm{s}):\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}
be defined by the integral

(23)

\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s}):=\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})\varphi(||x||y)|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(y).

This integral converges absolutely because
\varphi
is compactly supported. Note also that the integral is independent of the chosen representative of
||x||\in\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
because
\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-\bm{s}}
is invariant under multiplication by
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
.

Let
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(\bm{s}):=\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\varphi(z)|z|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(z)
be the Mellin transform of
\varphi
where
\bm{s}\in(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Remark 7.2
.

For any fixed choice of
\bm{s}\in(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{C}}
we can always choose a
\varphi
such that
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(\bm{s})\neq 0
. Since everything that follows will follow for any choice of
\varphi
satisfying the above assumptions we may always assume that
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(\bm{s})\neq 0
.

Lemma 7.3
.

(1)

The function
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s})
is continuous in the variable
x
and holomorphic in
\bm{s}
.

(2)

If
\log\mathbf{H}(x)\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
then
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s})=(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-\bm{s})\mathbf{H}(x)^{-\bm{s}}
.

(3)

There exists some
\ell\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s})=0
if
\mathbf{H}(x)-\ell\not\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
.

Proof.

For the first part note that the integrand of (
23
) is continuous in
x
and holomorphic in
s
and that the integral converges absolutely.

For the second, note that if
\varphi(||x||y)\neq 0
then by taking log norms we have that
\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}-\log\mathbf{H}(x)\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{*}
. This implies that
\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{*}
. Making the change of variables
z=||x||y
we get

\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s})=\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\varphi(||x||y)|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(y)=\mathbf{H}(x)^{-\bm{s}}\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\varphi(z)|z|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(z).

The last part follows from the compact support of
\varphi
.
∎

7.1.4.
Height zeta function

The height zeta function is defined as

Z(\bm{s},f):=\sum_{P\in T(F)}f(P)\mathbf{H}(P)^{-\bm{s}}

for
\bm{s}\in(\Pic(X;\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{C}}:=\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})\otimes\mathbb{C}
for which this sum convergences absolutely. All of the following equalities will follow if at least one of the sides converges absolutely. We will show at the end that this holds if
\mathrm{Re}(\bm{s})-\mathbf{2}\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
.

Twisting the torsor
\pi:Y\to U
by
\mathfrak{a}\in\HH^{1}(a,T_{\NS})
results in a
T_{\NS}
torsor
\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}:Y\to X
by Lemma
2.11
. It follows from
2.2
that the Zeta function has the following decomposition

(24)

Z(\bm{s},f)=\sum_{a\in H^{1}(F,\mathbf{T}_{\NS})}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(\bm{s},f)

where

Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(\bm{s},f):=\sum_{P\in\Theta(F)/T_{\NS}(F)}f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(P))\mathbf{H}(P)^{-\bm{s}}.

We note that
2.11
applied to the
\mathcal{O}_{v}
-integral model
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}\to\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{O}_{v}}
implies that
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(\bm{s},f)=0
for
\mathfrak{a}\not\in\prod_{v\not\in S}\HH^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{v},T_{\NS})
. There are thus only finitely many
\mathbf{a}
such that
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(\bm{s},f)\neq 0
so the sum (
24
) is finite.

By Lemma
7.3
and the product formula we get an equality

(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-\bm{s})Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(\bm{s},f):=\sum_{P\in\Theta(F)/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(P;\bm{s})f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(P))|P|^{\mathbf{2}}_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}.

This is thus a sum over the adelic function

\mathcal{G}(\cdot;\bm{s}):\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)\to\mathbb{C}:x\to\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s})f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x))|x|^{\mathbf{2}}_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}.

Note that this is well-defined because
\mathcal{H}(\cdot;{\bm{s}})
is invariant under
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
.

We will evaluate this sum by applying Poisson summation (
4
) to the locally compact Hausdorff group
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)
. Let us first check that the condition in Theorem
2.17
holds.

Lemma 7.4
.

There exists a pair
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
of functions as in Construction
4.25
with the following property.

For each place
v\in S
there exists a compactly supported continuous function
g_{v}:U_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
which is locally constant if
v
is non-archimedean and which is smooth if
v
is archimedean and a neighbourhood
V\subset\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
of
1
such that if
g_{v}:=f_{v}
for
v\not\in S
and
g:=\prod_{v}g_{v}:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
then

|\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;{\bm{s}})f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x))|x|^{\mathbf{2}}_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}|\to\mathcal{H}_{\varphi^{\prime}}(yx;{\mathrm{Re}(\bm{s})})g(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(yx))|yx|^{\mathbf{2}}_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}.

for all
x\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
,
y\in V
and
\mathrm{Re}(\bm{s})\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
.

Proof.

Let
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
such that
\psi(x)\leq\psi^{\prime}(wx)
for all
x\in\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
and
w\in W
where
W
is an open neighbourhood of the
\prod_{v}T_{\NS A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
. The existence of such functions reduces to the archimedean case where it is clear.

For each
v\in S
choose an open neighbourhood of the identity
V_{v}\subset\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})
with compact closure. Moreover, take
V_{v}\subset\Theta(\mathcal{O}_{v})
for
v
a non-archimedean place. By compactness we can choose
g_{v}:U_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
satisfying the assumptions such that for all
y_{v}\in V_{v}
and
x\in U_{A_{v}}(F_{v})
we have
|f_{v}(x_{v})|\leq g_{v}(x_{v}\pi_{a_{v}}(y_{v}))
.

For
v\not\in S
we let
V_{v}=\Theta(\mathcal{O}_{v})
. Then
f_{v}\circ\pi_{a}
is
V_{v}
invariant by assumption so
|f_{v}(\pi_{a}(x_{v}))|\leq g_{v}(x_{v}y_{v})
also for these places
v
.

The product
V^{\prime}:=\prod_{v}V_{v}\subset\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
has compact closure. It follows that there exist a
L>0
such that
L^{-1}\leq|y|^{\mathbf{2}}_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}
for all
y\in V
.

Consider the map
\Delta:\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times V\to\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\prod_{v}T_{\NS A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v}):(x,v)\to\Delta(x,y)=||x||_{v}||yx||_{v}^{-1}
. The map
\Delta
factors through
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times V^{\prime}
since it is invariant under
T_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
.

The map
\Delta
is continuous and sends
(x,1)
to
1
so by compact support of
g
there exists an neighbourhood of the identity
V\subset V^{\prime}
such that
\Delta(x,y)\in W
for all
y\in V
for which
g(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x))\neq 0
. By construction we have
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;{\bm{s}})\leq L\cdot\mathcal{H}_{\varphi^{\prime}}(yx;{\mathrm{Re}(\bm{s})})
.

The desired bound in the lemma then follows by taking by replacing
g_{v}
by
Lg_{v}
for one
v
.
∎

7.2.
The Fourier transform of
\mathcal{G}

In this subsection we will study the Fourier transform of the function
\mathcal{G}(\cdot;\bm{s})
. Let
\chi:\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a quasi-character and consider the Fourier transform

\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi;\bm{s}):=\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\chi\cdot\mathcal{G}(\cdot;\bm{s})d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}.

As a first step we substitute the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;\bm{s})
to get that this is equal

\begin{split}&\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\chi(x)f(\pi_{a}(x))|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{2}}\\
&\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})\varphi(||x||y)|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(y)d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}(x).\end{split}

This integral will only converge absolutely for
\bm{s}
sufficiently large. In this section we will always assume that
\bm{s}
is sufficiently large so that at least one of the sides of each equality converges absolutely. At the end we will show that such an
\bm{s}
does exist.

Make the change of variables
z=y^{-1}x
. Using that
\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}
is a Haar measure, Fubini, that
||z||=y||x||
and the fact that
f
is
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-invariant shows that
\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi;\bm{s})
is equal to

(25)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\chi(z)f(\pi_{a}(z))\varphi(||z||)|z|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{2}}d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}(z)\\
&\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\chi(y)\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\mathbf{2}-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(y).\end{split}

We will evaluate these two integrals separately. The inner integral can be evaluated explicitly

Lemma 7.5
.

The inner integral of (
25
) converges absolutely if
\mathrm{Re}(\bm{s})-\bm{2}-\mathrm{Re}(\chi)\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
.

If the restriction of
\chi
to
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{\NS}(F)
is non-trivial then

\int_{{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}}\mathbf{\chi}(y)\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\mathbf{2}-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(y)=0.

Otherwise
\chi=|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\bm{z}}
for some
\bm{z}\in\Pic(X,\mathbf{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
and

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathbf{\chi}(y)\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\mathbf{2}-\bm{s}}d\tau_{\NS}(y)=\\
\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}^{1}}(\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{\NS}(F))\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(\mathbf{X};\mathbf{A})}}(\bm{s}-\bm{2}-\bm{z}).\end{split}

Proof.

For absolute convergence we may replace
\chi
by
|\chi|
. This quasi-character is trivial when restricted to
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{\NS}(F)
since this group is compact by Lemma
3.28
. We can thus reduce to the second case.

We rewrite the relevant integral as

\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}}{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\mathbf{2}-\bm{s}}\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{\NS}(F)}\chi(yx)d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}^{1}}(x)d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}/\mathbf{T}^{1}_{\NS}}(y).

The inner integral is
0
if
\chi
is non-trivial when restricted to the compact group
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{\NS}(F)
by orthogonality of characters. Otherwise it is equal to
d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}^{1}}(\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/T_{\NS}(F))
.

The log norm
\log|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}
induces an isomorphism
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)^{1}\cong\Pic(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{A})^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}
by Lemma
3.27
, and this isomorphism sends the Haar measure
d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}/\mathbf{T}^{1}_{\NS}}
to the measure
du_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}
by construction.

This is a real vector space so every quasi-character
\Pic(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{A})^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is given by
e^{\langle\bm{z},\cdot\rangle}
for some
\bm{z}\in(\Pic(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{A}))_{\mathbb{C}}
. Every quasi-character of
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)^{1}
is thus equal to
e^{\langle\bm{z},\log|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}\rangle}=|\cdot|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\bm{z}}
.

We then compute that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}}{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{\mathbf{2}-\bm{s}+\bm{z}}d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}/\mathbf{T}^{1}_{\NS}}=\\
&\int_{\Pic(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{A})^{*}_{\mathbb{R}}}{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(u)e^{-\langle\bm{s}-\mathbf{2}-\mathbf{z},u\rangle}du_{\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})}.\end{split}

This is exactly the definition of
\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(\mathbf{X};\mathbf{A})}}(\bm{s}-\bm{2}-\bm{z})
.
∎

The integrand of the other integral is a product of the following local factors. For each quasi-characters
\chi_{v}:\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
define the local integral

\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v}):=\int_{\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})}\chi_{v}(x_{v})f_{v}(\pi_{a_{v}}(x_{v}))\varphi_{v}(||x_{v}||_{v})|x_{v}|^{\mathbf{2}}_{\Theta_{A_{v}}}d\tau_{\Theta_{A_{v}}}(x_{v}).

Every quasi-character
\chi
can be written as a product
\chi=\prod_{v}\chi_{v}
. By the definition of
\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}
we then have

(26)

\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\chi(z)f(\pi_{a}(z))\varphi(||z||)|z|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{2}}d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}(z)=\prod_{\rho\in D/\Gamma_{F}}\zeta^{*}_{F_{\rho}}(1)^{-1}\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}}\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v}).

7.3.
Local Fourier transforms

Let us first note the following fact

Lemma 7.6
.

The product function
Y_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}:x_{v}\to f_{v}(\pi_{a}(x_{v}))\varphi(||x_{v}||_{v})
is compactly supported for all places
v\in\Omega_{F}
and is smooth for
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
.

Proof.

The function
(\pi_{a_{v}},||\cdot||):Y_{A_{v}}(F_{v})/T_{\NS,v}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to X(F_{v})\times T_{\NS,v}(F_{v})/T_{\NS,v}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
is an open and closed immersion by Lemma
4.5
.

The function
X(F_{v})\times T_{\NS,v}(F_{v})/T_{\NS,v}(\mathcal{O}_{v})\to\mathbb{R}:(x_{v},t_{v})\to f_{v}(x_{v})\varphi_{v}(x)
is a product of compactly supported functions which are smooth if
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
and thus has those same properties. The lemma follows.
∎

Let
v\in\Omega_{F}
be a place. The torus
\Theta_{A_{v}}
is by definition the Weil restriction of the étale algebra corresponding to the
\Gamma_{v}
-set
D\cup A_{v}
. For
\rho_{v}\in(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}
let
F_{\rho_{v}}
be the field extension of
F_{v}
corresponding to the
\Gamma_{v}
-orbit
\rho_{v}
. We then have

\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\cong\prod_{\rho_{v}\in(R\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}}F_{\rho_{v}}^{\times}.

Under this identification the we have an equality of measures

(27)

\tau_{\Theta_{A_{v}}}(x)=\prod_{\rho_{v}\in(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}}\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(1)|x_{\rho_{v}}|^{-1}dx_{\rho_{v}}.

We can also make the following identification

\Hom(\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v}),\mathbb{C}^{\times})\cong\prod_{\rho_{v}\in(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}}\Hom(F^{\times}_{\rho_{v}},\mathbb{C}^{\times}).

This space naturally has the structure of a complex manifold
9
9

9

With a countable number of components.
.

Every quasi-character
\chi_{v}:\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
can thus be uniquely written as a product
\chi_{v}=\prod_{\rho\in(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}}\chi_{\rho_{v}}
where
\chi_{\rho_{v}}:F_{\rho_{v}}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is a quasi-character.

We can also conclude that every quasi-character
\chi_{v}:\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
can be written as a product
\chi_{v}=\tilde{\chi}_{v}|\cdot|_{v}^{\mathbf{z}}
for some
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}[(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}]
and character
\tilde{\chi}_{v}:\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to S^{1}
. Note that
\mathrm{Re}(\mathbf{z})\in\mathbb{R}[(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}]
is uniquely determined by the quasi-character
\chi
. We can choose
\tilde{\chi}=1
if and only if
\chi
is trivial when restricted to
\Theta_{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, in this case we say that
\chi
is
unramified
.

For
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}[(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}]
we define the
tube domain

\mathbf{T}_{>\mathbf{x}}:=\{\chi\in\Hom(\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v}),\mathbb{C}^{\times}):\mathrm{Re}(\chi)>\mathbf{x}\}.

The partial order on
\mathbb{R}[(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}]
is the components-wise one.

7.3.1.
Finite places

For non-archimedean places we will use the following lemma

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\emph{fin}}
. The Fourier transform
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})
converges absolutely and is holomorphic for
\chi_{v}\in\mathbf{T}_{>-\mathbf{2}}
. There exists a compact open subgroup
\mathbf{K}_{v}\subset\Theta(F_{v})
such that
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=0
if
\chi_{v}
is non-trivial when restricted to
\mathbf{K}_{v}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{K}_{v}\subset\Theta(\mathcal{O}_{v})
be the maximal subgroup such that
f_{v}
and
||\cdot||_{v}
are invariant under
\mathbf{K}_{v}
. This is an open subgroup because
f_{v}
and
||\cdot||_{v}
are locally constant.

By the usual argument via orthogonality of characters we conclude that
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=0
if
\chi_{v}
is trivial when restricted to
\mathbf{K}_{v}
.

Now write the quasi-character
\chi_{v}=\tilde{\chi}_{v}|\cdot|^{\mathbf{z}}
for some
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}[E/\Gamma_{v}]
. The holomorphicity of
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})
will follow from absolute convergence so we may assume that
\tilde{\chi}_{v}=1
. By (
27
) we have

\int_{\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})}f_{v}(\pi_{a_{v}}(x_{v}))\varphi_{v}(||x_{v}||_{v})|x_{v}|^{\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{z}}_{\Theta_{A_{v}}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}}\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(1)dx_{\rho_{v}}.

This integral converges absolutely as long as
\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{2}>0
by
[
CLT10a
, Prop. 3.4]
and Lemma
7.6
.
∎

We can do much better than the above lemma at the places of good reduction. We note the similarity of the following lemma to
[
CLT10b
, Lemma 3.2.2]
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
v\not\in S
. If
\chi_{v}
is ramified then
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=0
, if
\chi_{v}
is unramified and contained in the tube domain
\mathbf{T}_{>\mathbf{2}}
then

\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=\prod_{\rho_{v}\in\Gamma_{v}\setminus E}L_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(2;\chi_{\rho_{v}})\left(1+O\left(q_{v}^{-4-2\min_{\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}}\mathrm{Re}(\chi_{\rho_{v}})}\right)\right).

Proof.

The assumption
v\not\in S
implies that
x_{v}\to f_{v}(\pi_{a}(x_{v}))\varphi_{v}(x_{v})
is the indicator function of
\mathcal{Y}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
, it is in particular invariant under the action of
\Theta_{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
so
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=0
if
\chi_{v}
if
\chi_{v}
is ramified by orthogonality of characters.

If
\chi_{v}
is unramified then it is equal to
|\cdot|_{\Theta_{v}}^{\mathbf{z}}
for some
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}[\Gamma_{v}\setminus E]
. By (
27
) we have to compute

\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=\prod_{\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}}\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(1)\int_{\Theta(F_{v})\cap\mathcal{Y}(\mathcal{O}_{v})}|x|^{\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{z}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}}dx_{\rho_{v}}.

For each
\Gamma_{v}
-invariant subset
B\subset D
let
\mathcal{Z}_{B}:=\cap_{\beta\in B}\mathcal{Z}_{\beta}
be the intersection of the corresponding varieties, it is defined over
F
. The closed subscheme
\mathcal{Z}_{B}
is an intersection of toric divisors so the open subscheme
\mathcal{Z}_{B}^{\circ}:=\mathcal{Z}_{B}\setminus\cup_{\varepsilon\in D\setminus B}\mathcal{Z}_{\varepsilon}
is the torus
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D\setminus B}
if
B
is the set of rays of a cone in
\Sigma
and empty otherwise.

For
\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}
let
f_{\rho_{v}}
be the degree of the (necessarily unramified) extension
F_{\rho_{v}}/F
.

The étale extension
F_{v}[D\setminus B]/F_{v}
is unramified so
\#\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D\setminus B}(\mathbb{F}_{v})=\prod_{\rho_{v}\in(D\setminus B)/\Gamma_{v}}\mathbb{F}_{\rho_{v}}^{\times}=\prod_{\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}}(q^{f_{\rho_{v}}}-1)=\prod_{\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}}q^{f_{\rho_{v}}}\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(1)
.

Substituting this into Denef’s formula
[
CLT10b
, Prop. 4.5]
and simplifying we find the equality

\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma^{\Gamma_{v}}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in\sigma/\Gamma_{v}}(q_{v}^{f_{\rho_{v}}(2+z_{\rho_{v}})}-1)^{-1}.

The contribution of the trivial cone is
1
and the contribution of a ray
\rho_{v}\in D^{\Gamma_{v}}
is
q^{-2-z_{\rho_{v}}}+O(q^{-4-2\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho_{v}})})
. The contribution of higher dimensional cones
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\Gamma_{v}}
is
O(q^{-4-2\min_{\rho_{v}\in\sigma/\Gamma_{v}}(\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho}))})
. The lemma follows since
L_{\rho_{v}}(2,\chi_{v})=(1-q^{f_{\rho_{v}}(-2-z_{\rho_{v}})})^{-1}
.
∎

7.3.2.
Infinite places

When
v
is an archimedean then every quasi-character
\chi_{v}
of
F_{\rho_{v}}
can be written as a product
y\to y^{n}|y|^{z}
for unique
z\in\mathbb{C}
and
n\in\{0,1\}
or
n\in\mathbb{Z}
for
F_{\rho_{v}}\cong\mathbb{R}
or
F_{\rho_{v}}\cong\mathbb{C}
respectively. We define
||\chi_{v}||:=\max(|z|,n)
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
. The Fourier transform
\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})
converges absolutely for
\mathbf{T}_{>\mathbf{-2}}^{D\cup A_{v}}
and there exists a holomorphic function
\varpi_{A_{v}}:\mathbf{T}_{>\mathbf{-\frac{5}{2}}}^{D\cup A_{v}}\to\mathbb{C}
such that

\hat{G_{v}}(\chi_{v})=\varpi_{A_{v}}(\chi_{v})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho_{v}\in D/\Gamma_{v}\cup A_{v}\\
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=|\cdot|^{z_{\rho_{v}}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2+z_{\rho_{v}}}.

The function
\varpi_{A_{v}}
is rapidly decreasing in vertical strips in the sense that for every compact
C\subset\mathbb{R}_{>-\frac{5}{2}}[(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}]
and for all
\kappa>0
we have for
\chi_{v}
with
\mathrm{Re}(\chi_{v})\in C
that

\varpi_{A_{v}}(\chi_{v})\ll_{\kappa,C}(1+||\chi_{v}||)^{-\kappa}.

Proof.

Write the quasi-character as
\chi_{v}=\tilde{\chi}_{v}|\cdot|^{\mathbf{z}}
for
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}[D/\Gamma_{v}\cup A_{v}]
and
\tilde{\chi}_{v}
a character. We may moreover assume that if
\chi_{\rho_{v}}
is unramified then
\tilde{\chi}_{\rho_{v}}=1
. We then have by (
27
) that

\hat{G}_{v}(\chi_{v})=\int_{\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})}f_{v}(\pi_{a}(x_{v}))\varphi_{v}(||x_{v}||_{v})\tilde{\chi}_{v}(x_{v})|x_{v}|^{\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{z}}_{\Theta_{A_{v}}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in(D\cup A_{v})/\Gamma_{v}}dx_{\rho_{v}}

This is the Mellin transform of a smooth compactly supported function by Lemma
7.6
. The lemma thus follows from properties of Mellin transforms, c.f.
[
CLT10a
, Lemma 3.4]
and
[
CLT10b
, Lemma 3.4.1]
.
∎

7.3.3.
The global Fourier transform

For each place
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\text{fin}}
let
\mathbf{K}_{v}\subset\Theta(F_{v})
be a compact open group as in Lemma
7.7
. By Lemma
7.8
we may assume that
\mathbf{K}_{v}=\Theta(\mathcal{O}_{v})
for
v\not\in S
.

For
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
put
\mathbf{K}_{v}=1
. Define
\mathbf{K}:=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}}\mathbf{K}_{v}\subset\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
.

Any quasi-character
\chi:\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is equal to a product
\prod_{v}\chi_{v}
of quasi-characters
\Theta_{A_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, all but finitely many of which are trivial when restricted to
\Theta_{A_{v}}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
. We define
||\chi||=\max_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}||\chi_{v}||
.

Also, every quasi-character
\chi:\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
can be written as a product
\prod_{\rho\in D/\Gamma}\chi_{\rho}\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}}\chi_{\rho_{v}}
where
\chi_{\rho}:\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F_{\rho}})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
and
\chi_{\rho_{v}}:\mathbb{G}_{m}(F_{\rho_{v}})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
by the definition of
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}
.

We can combine the above lemmas to immediately deduce the following.

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
\chi
be a quasi-character
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. If
\mathrm{Re}(\chi)\in\mathbf{T}_{>(\mathbf{-\frac{3}{2}},\mathbf{-\frac{5}{2}})}
then the integral
\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi;\mathbf{s})
converges absolutely.

If the restriction of
\chi
to
\mathbf{K}
or
\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}
is non-trivial then
\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi;\mathbf{s})=0
.

In general there exist a holomorphic function
\eta_{\mathbf{A}}
on the tube domain
\mathbf{T}_{>-\frac{3}{2},>-\frac{5}{2}}
such that

\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi;\mathbf{s})=\prod_{\rho\in D/\Gamma}L_{F_{\rho}}(2;\chi_{\rho})\eta_{\mathbf{A}}(\chi)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\\
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=|\cdot|^{z_{\rho_{v}}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2+z_{\rho_{v}}}\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\mathbf{s}-\mathbf{2}-\bm{z}).

The function
\eta_{\mathbf{A}}
is rapidly decreasing in vertical strips in the sense that for any compact
C\subset\mathbb{R}_{>-\frac{3}{2}}[D/\Gamma]\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{R}_{>-\frac{5}{2}}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]
and for all
\kappa>0
we have for
\chi
with
\mathrm{Re}(\chi)\in C
that

(28)

\eta_{\mathbf{A}}(\chi)\ll_{\kappa,C}(1+||\chi||)^{-\kappa}.

The function
\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi;\mathbf{s})
thus has a meromorphic continuation to the tube domain
\mathbf{T}_{>-(\mathbf{\frac{3}{2}},\mathbf{\frac{5}{2}})}
.

7.4.
Poisson summation

We will now apply the Poisson summation formula. Let us first study the Pontryagin dual
(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\Theta(F))^{\vee}
.

By Lemma
3.27
we have an exact sequence

(29)

1\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta(F)\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\Theta(F)\xrightarrow{|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}}=\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}[D/\Gamma_{F}]\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]\to 1

The group
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta(F)
is compact so its Pontryagin dual is discrete. The measure dual to
\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}
is
\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta(F))^{-1}
times the counting measure.

The volume of this group has been computed in Proposition
4.30
, in the notation of the proposition we have
T^{\prime}=\Theta
,
E=\emptyset
and
A_{v}=A_{v}
. We have
\tau(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}})=1
by Lemma
2.7
and the definition of
\tau(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}})
so
\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}^{1}}(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta(F))=c_{\mathbf{A}}
.

The group
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}[D/\Gamma_{F}]\times\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]
is a power of
\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}
. The Pontryagin dual of
\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}
is
\mathbb{R}
, where
x\in\mathbb{R}
corresponds to the character
|\cdot|^{ix}
. The corresponding dual Haar measure on
\mathbb{R}
is
\frac{1}{2\pi}
times the Lebesgue measure.

A formal application of the Poisson summation (
4
) formula thus leads to the equality

(30)

(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-\bm{s})Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(\bm{s};f,\varphi)=c_{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\sum_{\chi\in(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(F))^{\vee}}\mathfrak{Z}(\chi;\bm{s}).

where

(31)

\mathfrak{Z}(\chi;\bm{s}):=\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in D/\Gamma_{F}\\
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=0\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\\
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})};\bm{s})d\frac{\bm{z}}{2\pi i}d\frac{\bm{w}}{2\pi i}

and

\displaystyle d\frac{\bm{z}}{2\pi i}:=\prod_{\rho\in D/\Gamma_{F}}d\frac{z_{\rho}}{2\pi i}

\displaystyle d\frac{\bm{w}}{2\pi i}:=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}}d\frac{w_{\rho_{v}}}{2\pi i}.

The subgroup of
(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(F))^{\vee}
consisting of characters which are trivial when restricted to
\mathbf{K}
is a finitely generated group.

The integrand decays sufficiently fast in vertical strips to ensure absolute convergence by (
28
) and since L-functions grow at worst polynomially in vertical strips, see e.g.
[
Lou18
, Lemma 3.1]
. The sum over
\chi
also converges absolutely for the same reason, noting that the conductors of the
\chi_{\rho}
are bounded since
\chi
is trivial when restricted to
\mathbf{K}
. Theorem
2.17
then holds by noting that this absolute convergence also holds if one replaces
\phi
and
f
by the
\phi^{\prime}
and
g
in Lemma
7.4
.

7.4.1.
Moving contours

Let
L\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
be a big line bundle,
a:=a((X,\mathbf{A});L)
and
C\subset\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
the minimal face containing the adjoint divisor
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}+aL
. Let
b:=b((X,\mathbf{A});L)
be the codimension of
C
.

Let
D_{\adj}\subset E
be the
\Gamma_{F}
-set of toric divisors of
U
whose divisor class lies in the face
C
. Similarly, for each place
v
let
A_{v,\adj}\subset A_{v}
be the
\Gamma_{F_{v}}
-set of toric divisors contained in the face
C
. The divisors
D_{\adj}
and
A_{v,\adj}
generate
C
since the toric divisors generate the effective cone.

Let
E:=D\setminus D_{\adj}
and
B_{v}:=A_{v}\setminus A_{v,\adj}
for all
v
.

The following lemma shows that
\mathfrak{Z}(\chi;\bm{s})
can be analytically continued.

Lemma 7.11
.

There exists an
\varepsilon>0
independent of
\chi
such that the function
s\to(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-sL)Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
has a meromorphic continuation to
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a-\varepsilon
. It is holomorphic in this region except for possibly a single pole of order at most
b
at
s=a
.

Let
\xi(f,s,\bm{z},\bm{w})
be the following sum of integrals for
\bm{z}\in\mathbb{C}[E/\Gamma_{F}]
,
\bm{w}\in\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{C}[B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]

(32)

c_{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(F))^{\vee}\\
\chi_{\rho}=1:\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}\\
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=1:\rho_{v}\in B/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}\\
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=0\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}\\
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})+(\bm{z}_{\adj},\bm{w}_{\adj})};sL)d\frac{\bm{z}_{\adj}}{2\pi i}d\frac{\bm{w}_{\adj}}{2\pi i}.

Where
\bm{z}_{\adj}=(z_{\rho})_{\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}}\in\mathbb{C}[D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}]
and
\bm{w}_{\adj}=(w_{\rho_{v}})_{\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}}\in\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\mathbb{C}[A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}]
.

It converges absolutely for
\mathrm{Re}(s)
sufficiently large and
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})>-1,\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})>-2
for
\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}
,
\rho_{v}\in B_{v}
respectively. The function

(s-a)^{b}\prod_{\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}}(z_{\rho}+1)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}\\
\rho_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}(w_{\rho_{v}}+2)\xi(f,s,\bm{z},\bm{w})

has a holomorphic continuation to the region
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a-\varepsilon,\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})>-1-\varepsilon,\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})>-2-\varepsilon
and its value at
s=a,z_{\rho}=-1,w_{\rho_{v}}=-2
is equal to
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-sL)Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
.

Proof.

Choose
0<\delta<\frac{1}{4}
such that

(33)

K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}+aL-\sum_{\rho\in D_{\adj}}2\delta[Z_{\rho}]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}\end{subarray}}2\delta[Z_{\rho_{v}}]\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}.

Such a
\delta
exists by the definitions of
D_{\adj}
and
A_{v,\adj}
.

For each
\chi\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(F))^{\vee}
move in the definition of
\mathfrak{Z}(\chi;sL)
the contours coming from
\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}
and
\rho_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}
to the lines
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=-\frac{5}{4}
and
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=-\frac{9}{4}
respectively. Moreover, also move the contours corresponding to
\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}
and
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}
to the lines
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=-1+\delta
and
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=-2+\delta
respectively.

By Lemma
7.10
each contour moved this way only meets at most a single pole at
z_{\rho}=1
and
w_{\rho_{v}}=0
respectively. And it only meets such a pole if
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=1
and
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=1
respectively.

By the residue theorem
\mathfrak{Z}(\chi;sL)
is thus equal to the sum over all possible subsets
I\subset E/\Gamma_{F}
and
J_{v}\subset B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}
for all
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
such that
\chi_{\rho}=1
for all
\rho\in I
and
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=1
for all
\rho_{v}\in J_{v}
of the following terms

(34)

\begin{split}&\prod_{\rho\in I}\zeta^{*}_{{}_{F_{\rho}}}(1)\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=-\frac{5}{4}:\rho\in I^{c}\\
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=-1+\delta:\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}\end{subarray}}\prod_{\rho\in I^{c}\cup(D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F})}L(2+z_{\rho};\chi_{\rho})\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=-\frac{9}{4}:\rho_{v}\in J_{v}^{c}\\
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=-2+\delta:\rho\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}\\
&\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in J_{v}\cup(B_{v}/\Gamma_{v})\\
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2+w_{\rho_{v}}}\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(sL-\mathbf{2}-(\bm{z},\bm{w}))\eta_{\mathbf{A}}(|\cdot|^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})}\cdot\chi)d\frac{\bm{z}}{2\pi i}d\frac{\bm{w}}{2\pi i}.\end{split}

Here
z_{\rho}=1
and
w_{\rho_{v}}=1
for
\rho\in I
and
\rho_{v}\in J_{v}
respectively.

The integrand decays sufficiently fast in vertical strips for the same reason as for
\mathfrak{Z}(\chi;\bm{s})
. Similarly, the sum over characters
\chi
of (
34
) converges absolutely.

The integrand is a meromorphic function in
s
and the only part of the integrand which depends on
s
is the factor
\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(sL-\mathbf{2}-(\bm{z},\bm{w}))
so let us analyse this.

By the identity (
22
)
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}=(-\mathbf{1},\mathbf{0})
we have

\begin{split}sL-\mathbf{2}-(\bm{z},\bm{w})=&(s-a)L+K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}+aL-\sum_{\rho\in D_{\adj}}2\delta[Z_{\rho}]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}\end{subarray}}2\delta[Z_{\rho_{v}}]\;+\\
&\sum_{\rho\in I^{c}}(-z_{\rho}-1)[Z_{\rho}]+\sum_{\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}}(-z_{\rho}-1+2\delta)[Z_{\rho}]\;+\\
&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in J_{v}^{c}\end{subarray}}(-w_{\rho_{v}}-2)[Z_{\rho_{v}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}(-w_{\rho_{v}}-2+2\delta)[Z_{\rho_{v}}].\end{split}

Let
C_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}\subset\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
be the face generated by all
[Z_{\rho}]
for
\rho\not\in I
and
[Z_{\rho_{v}}]
for
\rho_{v}\not\in J_{v}
. We clearly have
C\subset C_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}
. Moreover we have
C=C_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}
if and only if
I=\emptyset,J_{v}=\emptyset
since
[Z_{\rho}],[Z_{\rho_{v}}]\not\in C
if
\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F},\rho_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}
by the definition of
E
and
B_{v}
respectively.

Let
b_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}
be the codimension of
C_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}
. By the above we have
b\geq b_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}
with equality if and only
I=\emptyset,J_{v}=\emptyset

Note that in the integral (
34
) we have
\mathrm{Re}(-z_{\rho}-1),\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho}-1+2\delta),\mathrm{Re}(-w_{\rho_{v}}-2),\mathrm{Re}(-w_{\rho_{v}}-2+2\delta)>0
for
\rho\in I^{c},\rho\in E/\Gamma_{\adj},\rho_{v}\in J_{v}^{c},\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}
respectively.

In the notation of Lemma
2.16
the linear function
sL-\mathbf{2}-(\bm{z},\bm{w})
is thus equal to a sum of
(s-a)L
and an element in
C_{I,(J_{v})_{v},\mathbb{C}}^{\circ}
. The lemma then implies that there exists a
\varepsilon>0
such
\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(sL-\mathbf{2}-(\bm{z},\bm{w}))
has a meromorphic continuation to the region
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a-\varepsilon
which is holomorphic except for a single pole at
s=a
of order at most
b_{I,(J_{v})_{v}}
. It follows that the same is true for the integral (
34
).

The integral
\xi(f,s,\bm{z},\bm{w})
converges and has a meromorphic continuation for the same reason.

By moving the contours corresponding to
z_{\rho}
for
\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}
and
w_{\rho_{v}}
for
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}
back we see that the contribution of (
34
) for the case
I=J_{v}=\emptyset
is equal to the value at
z_{\rho}=-1
for
\rho\in E/\Gamma_{v}
and
w_{\rho_{v}}=-2
for
\rho_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}
of the meromorphic continuation of

\prod_{\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}}(z_{\rho}+1)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}\\
\rho_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}(w_{\rho_{v}}+2)\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in D_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}\\
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=0\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}\\
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})+(\bm{z}_{\adj},\bm{w}_{\adj})};sL)d\frac{\bm{z}_{\adj}}{2\pi i}d\frac{\bm{w}_{\adj}}{2\pi i}.

We can switch the limit with the sum over characters by absolute convergence.
∎

Corollary 7.12
.

The sum
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
is absolutely convergent for
s>a
and has a meromorphic continuation to the half place
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a-\varepsilon
with at most a single pole at
s=a
of order
b
.

Proof.

By Remark
7.2
we may assume that
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-sL)\neq 0
for any particular
s
by changing
\varphi
. This then follows from Lemma
7.11
.
∎

8.
The leading constant

In this section we will finish our analysis by determining the value of
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
. This is closely related to the leading constant. We keep the notation of the last section.

8.1.
Geometry

Consider the adelic tori
\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}:=(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}},(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}})_{v})
and
\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}:=(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}),(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E\cup B_{v}})_{v})
. There are clearly split exact sequence of locally compact groups

(35)

\displaystyle 1\to\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to 1

(36)

\displaystyle 1\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(F)\to\Theta(F)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F)\to 1

Let
\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}:=\ker(\mathbf{T}_{\NS}\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F}))=\mathbf{T}_{\NS}\cap\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}
.

Let
N_{L}\subset N
, resp.
N_{B_{v}}
be the full sublattice of
N
generated by the rays
\rho
, resp. the rays
\rho_{v}
, for
\rho\in E
, resp.
\rho_{v}\in E\cup B_{v}
. Let
M_{L}:=\Hom(N_{L},\mathbb{Z})
, resp.
M_{B_{v}}:=\Hom(N_{A_{v}})
, be the dual lattice. Let
M_{\adj}:=\ker(M\to M_{L})
, resp.
M_{v,\adj}:=\ker(M\to M_{B_{v}})
.

The following lemma can be compared with the diagram
[
BT96
, p. 3225]

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
\Pic U_{L,B_{v}}:=\coker(\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}]\to\Pic U_{A_{v},\overline{F_{v}}})
. The following commutative diagram is well-defined. It has exact rows, exact columns, and the top vertical maps are injective.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{v,\adj}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\emph{Im}(\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}]\to\Pic U_{A_{v},\overline{F_{v}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}[D\cup A_{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pic U_{A_{v},\overline{F_{v}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M_{B_{v}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}[E\cup B_{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pic U_{L,B_{v},\overline{F_{v}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Moreover,
M_{L}=M_{B_{v}},M_{\adj}=M_{v,\adj}
and
N_{L}=N_{B_{v}}
for all places
v
.

Proof.

Exactness of the columns is by definition.

Exactness of the second row is a special case of (
2
) since
\overline{F}[U_{A_{v}}]^{\times}\subset\overline{F}[U]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}
.

Note that
M_{B_{v}}
is the image of
M
along the projection
\mathbb{Z}[D\cup A-v]\to\mathbb{Z}[E\cup B_{v}]
by definition. It follows that
M_{v,\adj}=\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}]\cap M
. This implies that the first row is exact. The third is then exact by the snake lemma.

If
M_{\adj}\neq M_{v,\adj}
then by the above there exists an element
m\in M
whose image in
\mathbb{Z}[D\cup A_{v}]
lies in
\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v}]
but not in
\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}]
. This implies that the rational function defined by
m
has a pole or zero at a divisor of
B_{v}
and all other poles and divisors on
U_{A_{v}}
are contained in
D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}
.

Taking Galois norms and divisor classes we find that the image in
\Pic(X,\mathbf{A})
of a non-trivial linear combination of elements in
B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}
is contained in
C_{\mathbb{R}}
. Since
C
is a face of
\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
the pullback along
\mathbb{Z}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]\to\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
is a face of the standard cone
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]
. But this implies that the divisor class of an element of
B_{v}
lies in
C
, which contradicts the definition of
C
.

We conclude that
M_{\adj}=M_{v,\adj}
. The rest of the lemma follows.
∎

Let
T_{L}\subset T
be the torus dual to
M_{L}=M_{B_{v}}
. Let
\Sigma_{L}
, resp.
\Sigma_{B_{v}}
be the subfan of
\Sigma
of the cones all of whose rays are elements of
E
, resp.
E\cup B_{v}
. This defines a regular fan on
N_{L}
and thus a smooth toric subvariety
T_{L}\subset U_{L}\subset U
, resp.
U_{L}\subset U_{L,B_{v}}\subset U_{A_{v}}
since Galois descent is effective for closed immersions.

Note that the
\Pic U_{L,B_{v},\overline{F_{v}}}
appearing in Lemma
8.1
is the geometric Picard group of
U_{L,B_{v}}
by (
2
).

Let
(U_{L};\mathbf{B}):=(U_{L},(U_{L,B_{v}})_{v})
. We have the following properties.

Lemma 8.2
.

(1)

\overline{F}[U_{L}]^{\times}=\overline{F}^{\times}
.

(2)

\HH^{0}((U_{L};\mathbf{B}),\mathcal{O}_{(U_{L};\mathbf{B})})=F
.

(3)

\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}
is the Néron-Severi torus of
(U_{L};\mathbf{B})
.

(4)

The image of
aL
in
(\Pic(U_{L};\mathbf{B}))_{\mathbb{R}}
is equal to
-K_{(U_{L};\mathbf{B})}
.

Proof.

For the first statement use that
E
generates
N_{L}
by definition.

For the second statement it suffices to show that
N_{L}
is generated by non-negative linear combinations of elements of
E
and
B_{v}
for all places
v
. If this was not true there would exist a non-zero
m\in M_{L}
which maps to a non-zero element
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}[E\cup\bigcup_{v}B_{v}]\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}[D\cup\bigcup_{v}A_{v}]
. Taking Galois norms we get a non-zero element
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}[D/\Gamma_{v}\cup\bigcup_{v}A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]
.

The element
f
maps to
0
in
\Pic U_{L,B_{v},\overline{F_{v}}}
for all
v
, so the image in
\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
must be contained in
C_{\mathbb{R}}
by Lemma
8.1
. Since
f
is non-negative we must have
f\in C
. But
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}[E/\Gamma_{v}\cup\bigcup_{v}B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}]
so this contradicts the definition of
E
and
A_{v}
. We conclude by contradiction.

Because
\Pic U_{L,B_{v},\overline{F_{v}}}=\coker(\mathbb{Z}[D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}]\to\Pic U_{A_{v},\overline{F_{v}}})
we have that its dual group of multiplicative type is
\ker(T_{\NS A_{v}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}\cup A_{v,\adj}})
. This agrees with the definition of
\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}
.

The last statement follows from the definitions of
a,D_{\adj},A_{v,\adj}
and Lemma
8.1
.
∎

Let
\mathbf{Y}_{L}:=\mathbf{Y}\cap(\mathbb{A}^{E},(\mathbb{A})^{E\cup B_{v}})
. It follows directly from the definitions that the restriction of
\pi
to
\mathbf{Y}_{L}
factors through
(U_{L},\mathbf{B})
and that
\mathbf{T}_{\NS L}
acts on
\mathbf{Y}_{L}
. It is clear from Proposition
2.10
that
\pi:\mathbf{Y}_{L}\to(U_{L},\mathbf{B})
is a universal torsor.

Let
\mathcal{R}:=\coker(T_{\NS}(F)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F))
. For each
\mathfrak{a}\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{R}
the map
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}:\mathbf{Y}_{L}\to(U_{L},\mathbf{B})\cdot\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha):x\to\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x\alpha)
is a universal torsor since it is just a shift of
\pi
.

8.2.
Integral over universal torsor

We can rephrase the conditions
\chi_{\rho}=1
for
\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}
and
\chi_{b_{v}}=1
for all
b_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}
and
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
in the definition (
32
) of
\xi(s,f,\varphi)
as the statement that
\chi
is trivial when restricted to
\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
. This is equivalent to
\chi\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\vee}
by (
35
).

Completely analogously to the analysis in §
7.4
one works out that

\begin{split}&\int_{(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F))^{\vee}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})};sL)d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}^{\vee}}(\chi)=\\
&c_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{1}/\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(F))^{\vee}\\
\chi_{\rho}=1:\rho\in D/\Gamma_{F}\\
\chi_{\rho_{v}}=1:\rho_{v}\in B/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in E_{\adj}/\Gamma_{F}\\
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})=0\end{subarray}}\mathop{\int\cdots\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}\\
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})+(\bm{z}_{\adj,\bm{w}_{\adj}})};sL)d\frac{\bm{z}_{\adj}}{2\pi i}d\frac{\bm{w}_{\adj}}{2\pi i}.\end{split}

Where
c_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}:=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}2^{\#\{a_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{a_{v}}=\mathbb{R}\}}(2\pi)^{\#\{a_{v}\in A_{v,\adj}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{a_{v}}=\mathbb{C}\}}
.

We can substitute this into (
32
) to deduce that

(37)

\begin{split}\xi(s,f,\bm{z},\bm{w})=c_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\int_{(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F))^{\vee}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})};sL)d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}^{\vee}}(\chi).\end{split}

Where
c_{\mathbf{B}}:=\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}2^{\#\{b_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{b_{v}}=\mathbb{R}\}}(2\pi)^{\#\{b_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{v_{v}}=\mathbb{C}\}}
.

It follows from the exact sequences (
35
) and (
36
) that the following sequence is exact

1\to\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F)/T_{\NS}(F)\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)\to\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F)\to 1

The integral in (
37
) converges absolutely by Lemma
7.11
, it also still converges after replacing
\varphi
and
f
by the
\varphi^{\prime}
and
g
in Lemma
7.4
. By applying the Poisson summation formula, i.e. Theorem
2.17
to the function
\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F)/T_{\NS}(F)\to\mathbb{C}:x\to\mathcal{G}(x;sL)|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})}
we deduce that
\xi(s,f,\bm{z},\bm{w})
is equal to

(38)

\begin{split}&c_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\int_{(\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F))^{\vee}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}(\chi\cdot|\cdot|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})};sL)d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}^{\vee}}(\chi)=\\
&c_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F)/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathcal{G}(x;sL)|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})}d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}(x)\\
&\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{R}}c_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}\mathcal{G}(\alpha x;sL)|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}^{(\bm{z},\bm{w})}d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}(x).\end{split}

Where
\mathcal{R}:=\coker(T_{\NS L}(F)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{D_{\adj}}(F))
and the last equality follows since
|x\alpha|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}}}=|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}|\alpha|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{A}_{\adj}}}=|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}
by the product formula. This sum is thus absolutely convergent for
\mathrm{Re}(s)
sufficiently large and
\mathrm{Re}(z_{\rho})>-1
,
\mathrm{Re}(w_{\rho_{v}})>-2
.

We will relate these integrals to geometric integrals, the following lemma is inspired by
[
CLT10a
, Prop. 4.3.4]

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
g:\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}
be a compactly supported continuous function. We then have

\begin{split}&c_{\mathbf{B}}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}g(x)dx=\\
&\prod_{\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}}\lim_{z_{\rho}\to-1}(z_{\rho}+1)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
w_{\rho_{v}}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}\lim_{w_{\rho_{v}}\to-2}(w_{\rho_{v}}+2)\int_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS L}(F)}g(x)|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}^{\mathbf{2}+(\bm{z},\bm{w})}d\tau_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}(x).\end{split}

Proof.

By a standard density argument we may assume that
g=\prod_{v}g_{v}
where
g_{v}:Y_{L,B_{v}}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is a compactly supported function which is smooth, resp. locally constant, if
v
is archimedean, resp. non-archimedean.

For
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}
the local contribution from the right-hand side is

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}\\
\rho_{v}\in A_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}\lim_{w_{\rho_{v}}\to-2}(w_{\rho_{v}}+1)\int_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E\cup B_{v}}(F_{v})}g_{v}(x_{v})|x_{v}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E\cup B_{v}}(F_{v})}^{\mathbf{1}+(\bm{z},\bm{w})}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in(E\cup B_{v})/\Gamma_{v}}dx_{\rho_{v}}.

This is equal to
c_{B_{v}}\int_{Y_{L,v}(F_{v})_{B_{v}}}g(x_{v})|x_{v}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E\cup B_{v}}}^{\mathbf{1}+\bm{z}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in E/\Gamma_{v}}dx_{\rho_{v}}
by
[
CLT10a
, Prop. 3.4]
, where

c_{B_{v}}=2^{\#\{b_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{b_{v}}=\mathbb{R}\}}(2\pi)^{\#\{b_{v}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}:F_{v_{v}}=\mathbb{C}\}}.

For
v\in\Omega_{F}^{\text{fin}}
the local contribution in the right-hand side is

\int_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}(F_{v})}g_{v}(x_{v})|x_{v}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}}^{\mathbf{1}+\bm{z}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in E/\Gamma_{v}}\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(1)dx_{\rho_{v}}.

Taking the product over all places and taking into account the factor
\prod_{\rho\in E/\Gamma}\zeta^{*}_{F_{\rho}}(1)
shows that the right-hand side is the limit

\begin{split}c_{\mathbf{B}}\prod_{\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}}\lim_{z_{\rho}\to-1}&\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}}\int_{Y_{L,v}(F_{v})_{B_{v}}}g(x_{v})|x_{v}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E\cup B_{v}}}^{\mathbf{1}+\bm{z}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in E/\Gamma_{v}}dx_{\rho_{v}}\\
&\prod_{v\in\Omega_{F}^{\text{fin}}}\int_{Y_{L}(F_{v})}g_{v}(x_{v})|x_{v}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{E}}^{\mathbf{1}+\bm{z}}\prod_{\rho_{v}\in E/\Gamma_{v}}\frac{\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(1)}{\zeta_{F_{\rho_{v}}}(2+z_{\rho_{v}})}dx_{\rho_{v}}.\end{split}

This limit converges, which proves exactly what we had to prove.
∎

This allows us to compute the residue of
\xi(f,s,\bm{z},\bm{w})
.

Lemma 8.4
.

We have the equality

\begin{split}\prod_{\rho\in E/\Gamma_{F}}\lim_{z_{\rho}\to-1}(z_{\rho}+1)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Omega_{F}^{\infty}\\
w_{\rho_{v}}\in B_{v}/\Gamma_{v}\end{subarray}}\lim_{w_{\rho_{v}}\to-2}(w_{\rho_{v}}+2)\xi(f,s,\bm{z},\bm{w})=\\
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(\alpha x;sL)f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha x))dx.\end{split}

Proof.

Apply Lemma
8.3
to each term of (
38
). This application is valid since after expanding the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(x;sL)
the remaining integral over
\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}
has as integrand
f(\bm{\pi}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha x))\varphi(y||\alpha x||)|x|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{B}}}^{\mathbf{2}+(\bm{z},\bm{w})}
which is compactly supported on
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
and thus also on the closed subspace
\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
.
∎

8.3.
Anticanonical height

Lemma 8.5
.

Assume that
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}=-aL
. In this case
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
is equal to

\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))\mathbf{H}(z)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{H}^{-aL}}(z).

Proof.

We have
D=E
and
B_{v}=A_{v}
for all
v
since
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}=-aL
so
\mathbf{Y}_{L}=\mathbf{Y}
and
\mathcal{R}=1
. We will study the integral in the right-hand side of Lemma
8.4
.

Expanding out the definitions and making a change of variables as in §
7.2
we see that this is equal to

\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-sL-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(y)\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}}f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x))\varphi(||x||)dx.

The first integral is the only part which depends on
s
. This integral was computed in Lemma
7.5
, it is a constant times
\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(\mathbf{X};\mathbf{A})}}(sL-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})})
. The residue of
\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(\mathbf{X};\mathbf{A})}}(sL-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})})
at
a
is
\mathcal{X}_{\Eff_{(\mathbf{X};\mathbf{A})}}(L)
by Lemma
2.16
since
aL=K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
. We deduce that
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-sL)Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
is equal to

\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}})|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(y)\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}}f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x))\varphi(||x||)dx.

Make the change of variables
z=y\cdot x
and
t=y\cdot||z||
. We then have the equalities of measures
dx=|y|_{\Theta}^{-\mathbf{1}}dz
and
d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(t)=d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(y)
.

We have
|y|_{\Theta}^{-\mathbf{1}}=|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}=|y|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-aL}
by (
22
). By definition we also have
||z||=y^{-1}\cdot||x||
and
\left|t\cdot||z||^{-1}\right|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}=\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z)
. The above integral is thus equal to

\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z)^{-(a+1)L}dzd\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(t).

Now note that since
K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}=-aL
and the definition
\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z)^{-aL}dz
is equal to the Tamagawa measure
\tau_{\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z)^{-aL}}
. Note now that
\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z)=|t|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}\mathbf{H}(z)
, if one combines this with Lemma
6.8
we deduce that

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))\mathbf{H}(t\cdot z)^{-(a+1)L}dz=\\
&|t|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-aL}\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/T_{\NS}(F)}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))\mathbf{H}(z)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{H}^{-aL}}(z).\end{split}

Combining the above we see that
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-aL)\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
is equal to

\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\varphi(t)|t|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-aL}d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(t)\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))\mathbf{H}(z)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{H}^{-aL}}(z).

By Remark
7.2
we may assume that
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-aL)=\int_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\varphi(t)|t|_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}^{-aL}d\tau_{\mathbf{T}_{\NS}}(t)\neq 0
. The lemma follows after dividing both sides by
(\mathcal{M}\varphi)(-aL)
.
∎

Combining this lemma, Lemma
7.11
and (
24
) we deduce the following theorem.

Theorem 8.6
.

The series
Z(sL,f)
converges absolutely for
\mathrm{Re}(s)
sufficiently large. There exists an
\varepsilon>0
such that
Z(sL,f)
has a meromorphic continuation to the region
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a-\varepsilon
. Moreover, this meromorphic continuation is holomorphic up to a single pole of order
b
at
s=a
and
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}Z(sL,f)
is equal to

\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})}\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))\mathbf{H}(z)^{-L}d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{H}^{-aL}}(z).

This theorem implies that the integral Manin conjecture hold with our prediction for the constant, see Conjecture
6.6
.

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
L\in\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
be a line bundle such that
aL=-K_{(X;\mathbf{A})}
for
a>0
. Let
b=\emph{rk}\Pic(X;\mathbf{A})
. Let
H
be a height function on
L
, and
\mathbf{H}
an extension to
\mathbf{Y}
. Let
f:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}
be any compactly supported continuous function.

Assume that
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
is non-zero, which we define to be the finite sum

\frac{1}{a(b-1)!}\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})}\int_{\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}}(\log\mathbf{H}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z))H^{-1}(z)d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},H^{-a}}(z).

We then have as
T\to\infty
that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in T(F)\\
H(P)\leq T\end{subarray}}f(P)\sim c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)T^{a}(\log T)^{b-1}.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.14
it suffices to prove this for
\mathbf{H}
and
f
as in §7.1 This case follows from Theorem
8.6
and a Tauberian theorem, e.g.
[
Ten15
, Thm. II.7.13]
.
∎

Theorem
1.2
in the introduction follows from this theorem. Indeed, in that case
a=1
. Also
f
is non-negative and the measure
\tau_{\mathbf{Y},H^{-a}}
is supported on
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}
. So
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
will be non-zero as long as
f
is non-zero on any element of the image of
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}
along one of the
\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}
. This image is equal to
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br_{1}}
by Theorem
5.10
.

We can deduce the following corollary, implying Theorem
1.3
.

Corollary 8.8
.

The algebraic Brauer-Manin obstruction is the only obstruction to strong approximation for
(X;\mathbf{A})
.

Proof.

Let
V\subset(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
be an open such that
V\cap U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br_{1}}\neq\emptyset
. Let
f:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be continuous function with compact support
\text{supp}(f)\subset V
and such that
\text{supp}(f)\cap U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br_{1}}\neq\emptyset
. We have
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)\neq 0
by the same reasoning as above so Theorem
8.7
implies that
T(F)\cap V\neq\emptyset
.

We deduce that the closure
\overline{T(F)}
in
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
contains
U(\mathbb{A}_{F})_{\mathbf{A}}^{\Br_{1}}
.
∎

8.4.
Other heights

Let
L\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
now be any big height. For each
\alpha\in\mathcal{R}
let
H_{L,\alpha}
be the restriction of
H
to
(U_{L};\mathbf{B})
and
\mathbf{H}_{L,\alpha}
the height corresponding to an extension of the adelic metric defining
H
to the universal torsor
\mathbf{Y}_{L}\to(U_{L};\mathbf{B})
. This exists because of Lemma
4.12
.

Lemma 8.9
.

The limit
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
is equal to

\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(U_{L};\mathbf{B})}}(\log\mathbf{H}_{L,\alpha}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z\alpha))H_{L,\alpha}^{-1}(z)d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},H_{L,\alpha}^{-a}}(z).

Proof.

Let
W:=U\setminus\left(\cup_{Z_{i}\in D_{\adj}}Z_{i}\right)
and for each place
v
let
W_{v}:=U_{A_{v}}\setminus\left(\cup_{Z_{i}\in D_{\adj}\cup B_{c}}Z_{i}\right)
and let
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{U}
be the complement of the closures of the
Z_{i}
for
Z_{i}\in D_{\adj}
. Let
S\subset S_{1}\subset S_{2}\subset\cdots\subset\Omega_{F}
be a sequence of subsets of places such that
\cup_{n}S_{n}=\Omega_{F}
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
v\in\Omega_{F}
let
