g_{v,n}:W_{v}(F_{v})\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be a compactly supported function which is smooth, resp. locally constant, if
v
is archimedean, resp. non-archimedean, satisfying the following properties:
g_{v,n-1}\leq g_{v,n}\leq f_{v}
,
g_{v,n}
is the indicator function of
\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{O}_{v})
if
v\not\in S_{n}
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{v,n}=f_{v}
.

Let
g_{n}=\prod_{v}g_{n,v}
and
h_{n}=\left(\prod_{v\in S_{n}}f_{v}-\prod_{v\in S_{n}}g_{n,v}\right)\prod_{v\not\in S_{n}}f_{v}
. The function
g_{n}
, resp.
h_{n}
, is compactly supported continuous on
\prod_{v}W_{v}(F_{v})
, resp.
(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})
. Note that
g_{n}\leq f\leq g_{n}+h_{n}
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{n}=f
and
\lim_{n\to\infty}h_{n}=0
.

Let
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,g_{n})=\sum_{P\in T(F)}g_{n}(P)H(P)^{-sL}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,h_{n})=\sum_{P\in T(F)}h_{n}(P)H(P)^{-sL}
. Both integrals converge absolutely for
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a
by comparison with
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)
and Corollary
7.12
.

We claim that
\liminf_{s\to a,n\to\infty}|(s-a)^{b}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,h_{n})|=0
. Note that
h_{n}
can be written as a finite linear combination of functions as considered in §
7.1.2
. By Lemma
8.4
we find that the limit of
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,h_{n})
as
s\to a
is

\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}(\alpha x;sL)g_{n}(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha x))dx.

The above integral goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
by monotone convergence from which the claim follows. We deduce from
g_{n}\leq f\leq g_{n}+h_{n}
that

Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,f)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{s\to a}Z_{\mathfrak{a}}(sL,g_{n})
.

Note that
U_{L,B_{v}}\subset W_{v}
is a closed immersion. The function
g_{n}
is compactly supported on
\prod_{v}W_{v}(F_{v})
so for each
\alpha\in\mathcal{R}
its restriction to the closed subset
(U_{L};\mathbf{B})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)
is also compactly supported. Moreover, since it is compactly supported its restriction to
(U_{L};\mathbf{B})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)
is zero for all but finitely many
\alpha\in\mathcal{R}
.

By Lemma
6.4
we can apply Lemma
8.5
to
(U_{L},\mathbf{B})
and deduce that for all
\alpha\in\mathcal{R}
the limit
\lim_{s\to a}\sum_{P\in T_{L}(F)\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)}g_{n}(P)H(P)^{-sL}
is equal to

\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(U_{L};\mathbf{B})}}(\log\mathbf{H}_{L,\alpha}(z))g_{n}(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z\alpha))H_{L,\alpha}^{-1}(z)d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},H_{L,\alpha}^{-a}}(z).

We conclude by summing over
\alpha\in\mathcal{R}
, noting that this is a finite sum, and taking the increasing limit as
n\to\infty
.
∎

Combining this lemma, Lemma
7.11
and (
24
) we deduce the following theorem.

Theorem 8.10
.

The series
Z(sL,f)
converges absolutely for
\mathrm{Re}(s)
sufficiently large. There exists an
\varepsilon>0
such that
Z(sL,f)
has a meromorphic continuation to the region
\mathrm{Re}(s)>a-\varepsilon
. Moreover, this meromorphic continuation is holomorphic up to a single pole of order
b
at
s=a
and
\lim_{s\to a}(s-a)^{b}Z(sL,f)
is equal to

\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(U_{L};\mathbf{B})}}(\log\mathbf{H}_{L,\alpha}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z\alpha))H_{L,\alpha}^{-1}(z)d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},H_{L,\alpha}^{-a}}(z).

The same reasoning as for Theorem
8.7
shows that this implies the following.

Theorem 8.11
.

Let
L\in\Eff_{(X;\mathbf{A})}^{\circ}
be big. Let
H
be a height function on
L
, and
\mathbf{H}_{L,\alpha}
an extension of
H
to the universal torsor
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}:\mathbf{Y}_{L}\to(U_{L},\mathbf{B})\cdot\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)
. Let
f:(X;\mathbf{A})(\mathbb{A}_{F})\to\mathbb{C}
be any compactly supported continuous function.

Assume that
c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)
is non-zero, which we define to be the sum

\frac{1}{a(b-1)!}\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{R}}\int_{\mathbf{Y}_{L}(\mathbb{A}_{F})}\mathbf{1}_{\Eff_{(U_{L};\mathbf{B})}}(\log\mathbf{H}_{L,\alpha}(z))f(\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(z\alpha))H_{L,\alpha}^{-1}(z)d\tau_{\mathbf{Y},H_{L,\alpha}^{-a}}(z).

We then have as
T\to\infty
that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in T(F)\\
H(P)\leq T\end{subarray}}f(P)\sim c((X;\mathbf{A}),f,H)T^{a}(\log T)^{b-1}.

We deduce Theorem
1.1
by using the partition of unity arguments at the beginning of §6.

Remark 8.12
.

If
L
is adjoint rigid then
\pi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\alpha)\in T(F)=T_{L}(F)\subset(U_{L},\mathbf{B})
. The maps
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}:\mathbf{Y}_{L}\to(U_{L},\mathbf{B})
as
\mathbf{a}
ranges over all elements of
\HH^{1}(F,T_{\NS})
and
\alpha
ranges over all elements of
\mathcal{R}
then correspond to all universal torsors of
(U_{L},\mathbf{B})
by Lemma
5.9
. Theorem
8.11
thus agrees with Conjecture
6.6
in this case.
